\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]]

\displaystyle=-\pi^{\delta-1+2s}i\dfrac{\Gamma(-s)\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}+s\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{1}{-d+\delta+2s}\mathcal{F}[\partial_{1}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta+2s}]

\displaystyle=-\pi^{\delta-1+2s}i\dfrac{\Gamma(-s)\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}+s\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2\pi ix_{1}}{-d+\delta+2s}\dfrac{(2\pi)^{d-\delta-2s}\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}+s\right)}{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}2^{d-\delta-2s}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}-s\right)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2s}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2s-1}}{\kappa_{d,s}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}+s\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{d}{2}-s\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\delta}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2s}x_{1}.

Also, by the above computation, we observe that
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]]
coincides with a locally integrable function in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Overall, with
\overline{\varphi}_{\sigma}(x)=\varphi_{\sigma}(-x)
, we have

(4.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{1}(d,s,\delta)&\underset{\text{(3)}}{=}\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{1}\ast g_{2}},{\varphi_{\sigma}}\rangle}\underset{\text{(4)}}{=}\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}{\langle{\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]]},{\overline{\varphi}_{\sigma}}\rangle}\\
&\underset{\text{(5)}}{=}\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}{\langle{\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]]},{\overline{\varphi}_{\sigma}}\rangle}\\
&\underset{\text{(6)}}{=}-\frac{2^{2s-1}}{\kappa_{d,s}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}+s\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{d}{2}-s\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\delta}{2}.\end{split}

The proof is complete.
∎

By Remark
4.5
and Lemma
4.6
with (
4.7
), we have the following corollary.

Corollary 4.7

(Precise formula for
(-\Delta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}})^{s}u_{\delta}(x)
)
.

For each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there holds

\displaystyle(-\Delta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}})^{s}u_{\delta}(x)

\displaystyle\quad=\dfrac{2^{2s}\widetilde{c}_{d,s,\delta}}{4}\bigg[(d-\delta)\delta\bigg(1-\frac{1+2s}{2}\varepsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\frac{d+2s}{2}\bigg(2sf_{2,1}(d,s,\delta)-\frac{\widetilde{c}_{d,s,0}}{\widetilde{c}_{d,s,\delta}}\dfrac{s(d-1)(d-2s)}{d+2s}\bigg)\varepsilon\bigg]|x|^{1-2s-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1},

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}_{d,s,\delta}=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}+s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s+1-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}

and
f_{2,1}(d,s,\delta)
is defined in (
4.12
). Moreover,
(-\Delta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}})^{s}u_{\delta}(x)=0
if and only if

\displaystyle(d-\delta)\delta\left(1-\frac{1+2s}{2}\varepsilon\right)=\frac{d+2s}{2}\left(2sf_{2,1}(d,s,\delta)-\frac{\widetilde{c}_{d,s,0}}{\widetilde{c}_{d,s,\delta}}\dfrac{s(d-1)(d-2s)}{d+2s}\right)\varepsilon,

which is equivalent to

(4.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon&=\dfrac{2(d-2s+2-\delta)\delta}{(d-1)s(d-2s+2)-2(d-1)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s-\frac{\delta}{2}+2\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}+1\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}+s\right)}}\eqqcolon b(\delta).\end{split}

For
s\nearrow 1
, we have
\varepsilon\to\frac{d-\delta}{d-1}\delta
in agreement with (
1.6
).

Now we can show Theorem
4.2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
s\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
,
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
, and fix
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Note that for any
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
, we have
\lim_{s\nearrow 1}\widetilde{c}_{d,s,\delta}=1
. Then by Corollary
4.7
,

\displaystyle\lim_{s\nearrow 1}(-\Delta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}})^{s}u_{\delta}(x)

\displaystyle=\left[(d-\delta)\delta\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad-\frac{d+2}{2}\left(2f_{2,1}(d,1,\delta)-\dfrac{(d-1)(d-2)}{d+2}\right)\varepsilon\right]|x|^{-1-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}

holds. Here,

\displaystyle f_{2,1}(d,1,\delta)=\dfrac{3\delta^{2}-3d\delta+d^{2}-d}{2(d+2)}

so that

\displaystyle 2f_{2,1}(d,1,\delta)-\dfrac{(d-1)(d-2)}{d+2}=\dfrac{3\delta^{2}-3d\delta+2d-2}{d+2}.

Thus
\lim_{s\nearrow 1}(-\Delta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}})^{s}u_{\delta}(x)=\left[(d-\delta)\delta-(d-1)\varepsilon\right]|x|^{-1-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}=(-\Delta_{{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}})u_{\delta}(x)
.
∎

We now prove a lemma, which is crucial for the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Lemma 4.8
.

For
\varepsilon=b(\delta)
in (
4.16
), we have the following:

(a)

There exists a number
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(d,s)\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
such that the function
b(\delta)
defined in (
4.16
) is an one-to-one map from
[0,\delta_{0}]
to
[0,\frac{1}{2}]
.

(b)

Moreover, in the above range,
0<b(\delta)\leq c_{1}\delta
with some
0<c_{1}=c_{1}(d,s)
.

Proof.

Proof of (a).
First, note that
b(0)=0
. Furthermore, observe that if we define the following denominator in (
4.16
) as

\displaystyle b_{1}(\delta)=(d-1)s(d-2s+2)-2(d-1)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)\Gamma\left(2+\frac{d}{2}-s-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)},

then

(4.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}b_{1}(0)&=(d-1)s(d-2s+2)-2(d-1)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)\Gamma\left(2+\frac{d}{2}-s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s\right)}\\
&=\frac{2s^{2}}{d}(d-1)(d-2s+2)>0.\end{split}

For
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
we define the continuous function

(4.18)

\displaystyle L(\delta):=\dfrac{\Gamma\left(2+\frac{d}{2}-s-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}.

We claim that
L
is strictly increasing in
\delta
. For this we calculate

\displaystyle L^{\prime}(\delta)=\tfrac{L(\delta)}{2}\left(-\psi\left(2+\tfrac{d}{2}-s-\tfrac{\delta}{2}\right)+\psi\left(1+\tfrac{\delta}{2}\right)+\psi\left(1+\tfrac{d}{2}-\tfrac{\delta}{2}\right)-\psi\left(s+\tfrac{\delta}{2}\right)\right)>0,

where
\psi
is the digamma function. Now, the strict concavity of
\psi
implies that
\psi(a)+\psi(d)<\psi(b)+\psi(c)
for
a<b<c<d
and
a+d=b+c
. This and the positivity of
L
implies that
L^{\prime}(\delta)>0
. Hence,
L
is strictly increasing.

Now let
\delta_{1}\in(0,+\infty]
be such that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta_{1}=\inf\left\{\delta\in(0,+\infty]:(d-2s+2-\delta)\delta\geq 2b_{1}(\delta)\right\}.\end{split}

We consider two cases.

Case 1. When
\delta_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
:
By (
4.17
), (
4.18
), and (
4.19
), we have
b(\delta_{1})=\frac{1}{2}
, and
b(\delta)
is an one-to-one map from
[0,\delta_{1}]
to
[0,\frac{1}{2}]
. Thus we put
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(d,s)=\delta_{1}
.

Case 2. When
\delta_{1}>\frac{1}{2}
:
We will show that this case cannot occur. First, note that since
s\mapsto\Gamma(s)\left[\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)\right]^{-1}
is a decreasing function on
s\in(0,1)
, we observe

(4.20)

\displaystyle R_{0}(s,\delta):=\dfrac{\Gamma(s)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}+s\right)}\geq\dfrac{\Gamma(1)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}=1.

On the other hand, by
[
Alz97
, Theorem 10]
, the following is a decreasing function:

\displaystyle d\mapsto R(d):=\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s+1\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-s-\frac{\delta}{2}+2\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}+1\right)}.

Then by the asymptotic approximation
\Gamma(x+a)\sim\Gamma(x)x^{a}
for
x\to\infty
, we obtain
R(d)\geq\lim_{d\to\infty}R(d)=1
. Then together with (
4.20
), we see that
R_{0}(s,\delta)R(d)\geq 1
, so that for any
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
, there holds

(4.21)

\displaystyle\varepsilon=b(\delta)\geq\frac{2(d-2s+2-\delta)\delta}{(d\!-\!1)s(d\!-\!2s+2)-(d\!-\!1)(d\!-\!2s)s}=\frac{(d-2s+2-\delta)\delta}{s(d-1)}\geq\delta.

Since (
4.19
) implies
\varepsilon=b(\delta_{1})=\frac{1}{2}
, there is a contradiction between (
4.21
) and
\delta_{1}>\frac{1}{2}
. Thus
\delta_{1}>\frac{1}{2}
is not possible.

Proof of (b).
Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}=\min\{\delta_{0},\frac{1}{2}\}
. To prove (b), it remains to show that
b^{\prime}(\delta)\leq c_{1}
for any
\delta\in[0,\overline{\delta}_{0}]
with some
c_{1}=c_{1}(d,s)
. To do this, we see that

(4.22)

\displaystyle b^{\prime}(\delta)=\dfrac{2(d-2s+2-2\delta)b_{1}(\delta)-2\delta(d-2s+2-\delta)b^{\prime}_{1}(\delta)}{(b_{1}(\delta))^{2}}

holds. Now for
\delta\in[0,\overline{\delta}_{0}]
, we claim that

(4.23)

\displaystyle b_{1}(\delta)\geq c(d,s)>0.

Indeed, when
\delta\in[0,\overline{\delta}_{0}]
, by (
4.17
), (
4.18
) and (
4.19
), there holds

\displaystyle b_{1}(\delta)\geq b_{1}(\overline{\delta}_{0})\geq 2(d-2s+2-\overline{\delta}_{0})\overline{\delta}_{0}(1+2s)=c(d,s)>0.

Also, since
\delta\mapsto 2(d-2s+2-2\delta)b_{1}(\delta)-2\delta(d-2s+2-\delta)b^{\prime}_{1}(\delta)
is continuous on
\delta\in[0,\overline{\delta}_{0}]
, it is bounded above, so that

(4.24)

\displaystyle{\lvert 2(d-2s+2-2\delta)b_{1}(\delta)-2\delta(d-2s+2-\delta)b^{\prime}_{1}(\delta)\rvert}\leq c(d,s)

for any
\delta\in[0,\overline{\delta}_{0}]
. Considering (
4.23
) and (
4.24
) with (
4.22
), we see that
b^{\prime}(\delta)\leq c_{1}
for some
c_{1}=c_{1}(d,s)
. Therefore, by
0<b^{\prime}(\delta)\leq c_{1}
, (b) is proved.
∎

Now we provide the proof of one of our main results, Theorem
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
s\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
, and
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
. Since
s-\frac{d}{2}<1-\delta
for the given range of
d,s
and
\delta
, we see that
u\in W^{s,2}(B_{1})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle u\in W^{t,q}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(B_{1}(0))\,\,\text{for}\,\,1-\delta>t-\tfrac{d}{q}\quad\text{and}\quad u\not\in W^{t,q}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(B_{1}(0))\,\,\text{for}\,\,1-\delta\leq t-\tfrac{d}{q}.

In conclusion, (a) follows.

Note that
{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}(x)=\left(1-\frac{1+2s}{2}\varepsilon\right)\mathrm{Id}+\frac{d+2s}{2}\varepsilon\widehat{x}\otimes\widehat{x}
has

•

eigenvalue
1+\frac{d-1}{2}\varepsilon
with eigenvector
\widehat{x}

•

and eigenvalue
1-\frac{1+2s}{2}\varepsilon
with eigenspace
(\text{span}\{\widehat{x}\})^{\perp}
.

Hence for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
,
{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}(x)
is uniformly elliptic.

Also,
{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}(x)^{-1}=\frac{2}{2-(1+2s)\varepsilon}\mathrm{Id}-\frac{2(d+2s)\varepsilon}{(2-(1+2s)\varepsilon)(2+(d-1)\varepsilon)}\widehat{x}\otimes\widehat{x}
has

•

eigenvalue
\frac{2}{2+(d-1)\varepsilon}
with eigenvector
\widehat{x}

•

and eigenvalue
\frac{2}{2-(1+2s)\varepsilon}
with eigenspace
(\text{span}\{\widehat{x}\})^{\perp}
.

Then for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
, we have
|{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}(x)||{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}(x)^{-1}|\leq\left(1+\tfrac{d-1}{2}\varepsilon\right)\tfrac{2}{2-3\varepsilon}\leq d+3
, where for the second inequality we evaluate
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}
. We calculate

\displaystyle\log{\mathbb{A}}_{s,\varepsilon}(x)=\log\big(1-\tfrac{1+2s}{2}\varepsilon\big)\mathrm{Id}+\log\big(1-\tfrac{(d+2s)\varepsilon}{2+(d-1)\varepsilon}\big)\hat{x}\otimes\hat{x}

similar to
[
BDGN22
, Example 24]
. This implies
(
4.2
), so (b) is proved. Now (c) is from Lemma
4.8
together with Lemma
3.1
. The proof is completed.
∎

5.
Nonlocal equations with Riesz fractional derivatives

In this section we provide a Meyers-type example for a different fractional model. It is based on the fractional derivative
\nabla^{s}u
defined by the Riesz potential as introduced in
[
SS15
]
. This approach allows to define a fractional
p
-Laplacian as done in
[
SSS18
]
. Moreover, the use of
\nabla^{s}u
allows to consider even concepts like quasi-convexity, see
[
KS22
]
.

We proceed as in
[
SS15
]
. Let
0<s<1
and
1<p<\infty
. For
0<\alpha<d
let
I_{\alpha}u
denote the Riesz potential defined by
I_{\alpha}u=I_{\alpha}*u
, where

\displaystyle I_{\alpha}(x):=2\kappa_{d,\frac{-\alpha}{2}}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d-\alpha}}=\dfrac{2^{-\alpha}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\alpha}{2}\right)}{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d-\alpha}}.

This definition extends to tempered distributions. Then the
s
-fractional gradient is defined by

(5.1)

\displaystyle\nabla^{s}u=I_{1-s}(Du)=D(I_{1-s}u)

in the sense of tempered distributions. Now, the fractional Sobolev space
L^{s,p}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
is defined as the space with
u,\nabla^{s}u\in L^{p}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
. This definition corresponds to the Triebel-Lizorkin space
F^{s}_{p,2}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
. Thus,
L^{s,2}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})=W^{s,2}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
, but
L^{s,p}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})\neq W^{s,p}({\mathbb{R}^{n}})
if
p\neq 2
.

We define the fractional partial derivatives as
\frac{\partial^{s}u}{\partial x_{j}^{s}}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}(I_{1-s}u)
. Then the fractional divergence
\operatorname{div}^{s}w
is defined as
\operatorname{div}^{s}w=\sum_{j}\frac{\partial^{s}w_{j}}{\partial x_{j}^{s}}
for a vector field
w
. For
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
we define as in
[
Mey63
]

(5.2)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x)\coloneqq(1-\varepsilon)\mathrm{Id}+\varepsilon\widehat{x}\otimes\widehat{x}.

By some basic properties of
{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}
in
[
BDGN22
, Example 24]
, there hold

|{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x)||{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{-1}|\leq(1-\varepsilon)^{-1}
and
{\lVert\log{\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}\rVert}_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\varepsilon
.

We will now construct functions
u_{s,\delta}
such that

(5.3)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}^{s}({\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x)\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x))=0.

The idea is simple. Using (
5.1
) we formally have

(5.4)

\displaystyle-I_{1-s}\operatorname{div}\big({\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}\nabla(I_{1-s}u_{s,\delta})\big)=0.

If
v:=I_{1-s}u_{s,\delta}
, then this simplifies formally to

(5.5)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}\big({\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}\nabla v\big)=0.

This is exactly the equation used by Meyers
[
Mey63
]
. From
[
BDGN22
, Example 24]
we know that
v(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}\hat{x}_{1}
is a solution of (
5.5
) if
\varepsilon=\frac{d-\delta}{d-1}\delta
. Thus, we only need to find a function
u_{s,\delta}
such that
I_{1-s}u_{s,\delta}=v
. This can be formally done by

\displaystyle u_{s,\delta}=\Delta I_{2}u_{s,\delta}=\Delta I_{1+s}I_{1-s}u_{s,\delta}=\Delta I_{1+s}v.

Note that the homogeneity of
v
is
1-\delta
. Thus the one of
u_{s,\delta}
is
s-\delta
. The following theorem makes this idea more rigorous.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
, and
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\delta\in(0,\frac{d}{2})
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then the following holds:

(a)

u_{s,\delta}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}\hat{x}_{1}\in W^{s,2}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(B_{1}(0))
satisfies

\displaystyle u_{s,\delta}\in W_{{\mathrm{loc}}}^{t,q}(B_{1}(0))\,\,\text{if and only if}\,\,s-\delta>t-\tfrac{d}{q}.

(b)

Let
\varepsilon=\frac{d-\delta}{d-1}\delta
and let
{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}
be as in (
5.2
). Then
u_{s,\delta}
solves (
5.3
) in the sense of tempered distributions. Moreover, for all
\xi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}^{d}})
(Schwartz space),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}\nabla^{s}u\cdot\nabla^{s}\xi\,dx

\displaystyle=0.

The proof of this theorem is given at the end of this section.

Remark 5.2
.

We mention that when
d=2
, as
\delta
goes to
\frac{d}{2}=1
,
u
only satisfies
u\in W^{s,2}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(B_{1}(0))
and
u\notin W^{s+\epsilon,2+\epsilon}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(B_{1}(0))
for any fixed
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. This feature also appeared in the example given by Meyers
[
Mey63
]
.

Together with

\displaystyle I_{1-s}(x):=2\kappa_{d,\frac{-1+s}{2}}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d-1+s}}=\dfrac{2^{-1+s}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-1+s}{2}\right)}{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1-s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d-1+s}},

where
\kappa_{d,s}
is defined in (
2.2
), we define

(5.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(I_{1-s}\ast u_{s,\delta})(x)&:=2\kappa_{d,\frac{-1+s}{2}}\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert x-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{x_{1}-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\\
&=2\kappa_{d,\frac{-1+s}{2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{1-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh,\end{split}

following the definition in
[
SS15
, Definition 1.1]
. Note that the last expression in (
5.6
) is well-defined from Lemma
B.20
. Then we define the following.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
\delta\in(0,\frac{d}{2})
. Let
v\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
I_{1-s}\ast v
and
\frac{\partial^{s}v}{\partial x^{s}_{j}}
being well-defined. Then
v
is a distributional solution to

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}^{s}({\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x)\nabla^{s}v(x))\coloneqq-\sum^{d}_{i,j=1}\dfrac{\partial^{s}}{\partial x^{s}_{j}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x))_{ij}\dfrac{\partial^{s}}{\partial x^{s}_{i}}v=0\quad\text{in }B_{1}(0)

if for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1}(0))
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\varepsilon}(x)\nabla^{s}v(x)\cdot\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\,dx=0.

Note that since
d\geq 2
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\in(0,1)
, and
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
,
u_{s,\delta}\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Now we give necessary computations via Fourier transform.

Lemma 5.4
.

For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\delta\in(0,\frac{d}{2})
, we have

(5.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)&=c^{*}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}\left[(1-\varepsilon)^{2}e_{1}+(1-\delta-(1-\varepsilon)^{2})\widehat{x}_{1}\widehat{x}\right],\end{split}

(5.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x))\!=\!c^{*}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\left[-\delta(1\!-\!\delta)+(1\!-\!\delta\!-\!(1\!-\!\varepsilon)^{2})(d\!-\!1)\right]\widehat{x}_{1},\end{split}

and

(5.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}))(x)\\
&\quad=c^{**}\left[-\delta(1-\delta)+(1-\delta-(1-\varepsilon)^{2})(d-1)\right]{\lvert x\rvert}^{-s-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}\end{split}

a.e. sense on
{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
and in the sense of tempered distributions on
{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
, with

\displaystyle c^{*}=2^{-1+s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta+1}{2}\right)}\quad\text{and}\quad c^{**}=c^{*}2^{-1+s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{s+\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-s-\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}.

Proof.

From Theorem
B.25
, we can compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[I_{1-s}\ast u_{s,\delta}](\xi)

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{d,\frac{-1+s}{2}}\mathcal{F}[|x|^{-d-s+1}](\xi)\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{1}{s-\delta+1}\partial_{1}|x|^{s-\delta+1}\right](\xi)

\displaystyle=-ic^{*}\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}+\delta-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}\xi_{1}.

Then with the help of Corollary
B.26
, we calculate

\displaystyle(I_{1-s}\ast u_{s,\delta})(x)=(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[I_{1-s}\ast u_{s,\delta}](-x)

\displaystyle\quad=-ic^{*}\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}+\delta-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\left(\dfrac{-2\pi ix_{1}}{-d+\delta}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-\delta}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}\right)=c^{*}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}\widehat{x}_{1}.

Then
\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)=\nabla(I_{1-s}\ast u_{s,\delta})(x)=c^{*}|x|^{-\delta}(e_{1}-\delta\widehat{x}\widehat{x}_{1})
so (
5.7
) is proved. Then now it is straightforward to obtain (
5.8
). By Theorem
B.35
, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta})\right](\xi)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{2c^{*}\kappa_{d,\frac{-1+s}{2}}\left[-\delta(1\!-\!\delta)\!+\!(1\!-\!\delta\!-\!(1\!-\!\varepsilon)^{2})(d\!-\!1)\right]}_{=:c_{0}}\mathcal{F}[|y|^{-d-s+1}](\xi)\mathcal{F}\left[\tfrac{\partial_{1}|x|^{-\delta}}{-\delta}\right](\xi)

\displaystyle=-c_{0}i\pi^{s+\delta}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+1}{2}\right)\Gamma(\frac{d-\delta}{2})}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+s+\delta-1}\xi_{1}.

Then together with Corollary
B.36
, we have

\displaystyle(I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}))(x)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta})](-x)

\displaystyle=-c_{0}i\pi^{s+\delta}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+1}{2}\right)\Gamma(\frac{d-\delta}{2})}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-s-\delta}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{s+\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-s-\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-s-\delta-1}ix_{1}

\displaystyle=\left[-\delta(1-\delta)+(1-\delta-(1-\varepsilon)^{2})(d-1)\right]c^{**}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-s-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}

so (
5.9
) is proved.
∎

Note that
{\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}\widetilde{u}_{s,\delta}(x)
is
-\delta
-homogeneous and
I_{1-s}
is
(1-s)
-homogeneous, so
{\lvert(I_{1-s}\ast({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}\widetilde{u}_{s,\delta}))(x)\rvert}=\infty
is possible for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and so it may not be well-defined. Instead,
(I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}))(x)
is well-defined as a locally integrable function by Lemma
B.30
. Thus we prove the following.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
\delta\in(0,\frac{d}{2})
. For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}))(x)\varphi(x)\,dx.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in(0,1)
be given. From
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we know
{\lvert\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1
when
x\in B_{\sigma}(0)
. Then together with (
5.7
) we show that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}\,dx\lesssim\sigma^{d/2}.

Also, we have
{\lvert(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1
when
x\in B_{\sigma}(0)
. Then together with (
5.8
),

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma}\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta})(x)(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-1-\delta}\,dx\lesssim\sigma^{d-1-\delta}

holds. Furthermore, from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we know
{\lvert\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s}
when
x\in B_{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(0)
. Then together with (
5.7
) we observe that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s}\,dx\lesssim\sigma^{s}.

We have
{\lvert(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s+1}
when
x\in B_{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(0)
. Then with (
5.8
),

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma}}\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta})(x)(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s+1}\,dx\lesssim\sigma^{s}

holds. Now by divergence theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\right)(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma\}\cup\{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}\}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx.

Here, using (
5.7
), note that

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}\,dx\lesssim\sigma^{d-1-\delta}

which is from
{\lvert(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1
when
x\in B_{\sigma}(0)
, and

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s+1}\,dx\lesssim\sigma^{s},

which is from
{\lvert(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s+1}
when
x\in B_{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(0)
.

Considering the above seven observations, sending
\sigma\rightarrow 0
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\cdot\nabla^{s}\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\right)(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)\,dx.

Now using the fact that
I_{1-s}
is self-adjoint (or simply using change of variables),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\hskip-2.84526pt\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x)\right)(I_{1-s}\ast\varphi)(x)dx\!=\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\hskip-2.84526pt\left(I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbb{M}}_{\sigma}^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}\right)\right)(x)\varphi(x)dx

is obtained. The conclusion holds.
∎

Now we prove the main goal of this section.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
4.1
, with
[
BDGN22
, Example 24]
, (a) can be proved. For (b), by Lemma
5.5
, Lemma
B.14
together with Lemma
B.30
, it suffices to find the relation between
\varepsilon
and
\delta
which make
u_{s,\delta}
to solve
I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x))=0
pointwisely. Then to get
I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla^{s}u_{s,\delta}(x))=0
in a.e.
x\in B_{1}(0)
we need

\displaystyle-\delta(1-\delta)+(1-\delta-(1-\varepsilon)^{2})(d-1)=0\quad\iff\quad\varepsilon=1-\sqrt{1-\delta-\frac{\delta(1-\delta)}{d-1}}

from (
5.9
). Then we have
\varepsilon\leq C(d,s)\delta
for some
C(d)
. We complete the proof.
∎

We can also obtain the following robustness result as
s\nearrow 1
.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
, and
s\in(0,1)
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{s\nearrow 1}I_{1-s}\ast\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\nabla(I_{1-s}\ast u_{s,\delta}))(x)=\operatorname{div}({\mathbb{M}}_{\varepsilon}(x)^{2}\nabla u_{\delta}(x))

for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
\delta\in(0,\frac{d}{2})
.

Proof.

For each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, using (
5.9
) in Lemma
B.14
and
[
BDGN22
, Example 24]
, the above equality is equivalent to saying that
\lim_{s\nearrow 1}c^{**}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-s-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}={\lvert x\rvert}^{-1-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}
, which clearly holds since
\lim_{s\nearrow 1}c^{**}=1
. The proof is complete.
∎

Appendix A
Computations via Fourier transform

In this appendix, we give details in the proof of Lemma
4.6
which provides the computation of
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
given in (
4.13
). First, we define the distributions
g_{3}
and
g_{4}
in the proof of Lemma
4.6
, where the (formal) evaluation
x=e_{1}
to their convolution
g_{3}\ast g_{4}
will coincide to
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
.

Definition A.1
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define the distribution
g_{3}
and
g_{4}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle g_{3}:=j_{1}-j_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad g_{4}:=-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2},

where the distributions
j_{1},j_{2},\dots,j_{7}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
are defined as

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}h_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\begin{cases}-\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx&\,\text{if }d=2,\\
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx&\,\text{if }d\geq 3,\end{cases}

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{3}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}h_{1}^{3}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)x_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d\!-\!2s\!+\!2)(-d\!-\!2s)}\right)\partial_{1}\varphi(x)dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s}h_{1}^{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}x_{1}^{2}\varphi(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{5}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}h_{1}^{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}(2s\partial_{1}^{2}\varphi(x)-\Delta\varphi(x))}{2s(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s}h_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\partial_{1}\varphi(x)}{-d-2s+2}\,dx,

and

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{7}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\varphi(x)\,dx.

One can easily check that the above distributions are well-defined since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
{\lvert\partial^{k}_{i}\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{3}}\}
for
k=0,1,2,3
and
i=0,1,\dots,d
.

Now we obtain the precise computation of
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
.

Lemma A.2
.

We have

(A.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{3}](\xi)=\mathcal{G}_{3}(\xi)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}[g_{4}](\xi)=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s+1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\mathcal{G}_{4}(\xi)

in the distributional sense, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}(\xi):=\begin{cases}-i\pi^{\delta+1-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1}-2\pi\log|\xi|+2\pi(\log 2-\gamma)\,\,&\text{when }d=2\\
-i\pi^{\delta+1-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1}+\pi^{2-\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)|\xi|^{-d+2}\,\,&\text{when }d\neq 2\end{cases}

with
\gamma=-\Gamma^{\prime}(1)
the Euler-Mascheroni constant, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\dfrac{-2i(1-s)}{\pi(d+2s)}|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{3}+\dfrac{s-1}{2\pi^{2}}|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{2}-\dfrac{2}{d+2s}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{i(d+2s+3)}{\pi(d+2s)}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}+\dfrac{d+2s-1}{4\pi^{2}}|\xi|^{2s-2}-\dfrac{1}{s(d+2s)}|\xi|^{2s}

are locally integrable distributions.

Proof.

For
g_{3}
, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-\delta-2}h_{1}\right](\xi)

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{i}{2\pi}\right)\partial_{1}\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-\delta-2}\right](\xi)=-i\pi^{\delta+1-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1},

and with the help of
[
CW19
, Remark 1.2]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-2}\right]=\begin{cases}\pi^{2-\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)|\xi|^{-d+2}&\quad d\neq 2\\
-2\pi\log|\xi|+2\pi(\log 2-\gamma)&\quad d=2\end{cases}

in the distributional sense. For
g_{4}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{-h_{1}^{3}}{|h|^{d+2s+2}}\right]

\displaystyle=-\left(\dfrac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{3}\partial_{1}^{3}\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-d-2s-2}\right]

\displaystyle=i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{1}{d+2s}\left(2(s-1)|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{3}+3|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{h_{1}^{2}}{2|h|^{d+2s}}\right]=\dfrac{1}{4}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\left(2(s-1)|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{2}+|\xi|^{2s-2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{h_{1}^{2}}{|h|^{d+2s+2}}\right]=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\frac{1}{d+2s}\left(2|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{s}|\xi|^{2s}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{-h_{1}}{|h|^{d+2s}}\right]=i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{1}{2|h|^{d+2s-2}}\right]=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-2}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s+1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{d+2s-2}{4}|\xi|^{2s-2}.

Merging the terms calculated above, we obtain (
A.1
).
∎

Now we provide the formal evaluation
x=e_{1}
of
g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}
using approximation to identity, which is precisely computed by Fourier transform. We denote by
\varphi_{\sigma}
an approximation to the identity at
e_{1}
, i.e., for
\sigma\in(0,1)

\varphi_{\sigma}(x):=\frac{1}{\sigma^{d}}\varphi\left(\frac{x-e_{1}}{\sigma}\right)
for some standard mollifier
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Lemma A.3
.

We have

\displaystyle f_{2}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]]},{\overline{\varphi}_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}

\displaystyle\!=\!\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s\!+\!1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}f_{2,1}(d,s,\delta)\!-\!\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s\!+\!1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{d\!-\!1}{d\!+\!2s}.

Proof.

The first equality is given in Corollary
B.16
, so we only need to prove the second and the last inequality. Since
\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-2}\right]
in
\mathcal{F}\left[g_{3}\right]
is computed in different ways when
d\geq 3
and
d=2
, we divide the computation in two cases.

In case of
d\geq 3
:
By Theorem
B.15
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}](\xi)=\mathcal{F}[g_{3}](\xi)\mathcal{F}[g_{4}](\xi)=\frac{\pi^{2s}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s+1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\sum_{j=1}^{12}q_{j}(\xi),

where denoting
c_{2}=\pi^{\delta}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)/\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)
,

\displaystyle q_{1}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{-4(1-s)}{d+2s}c_{2}|\xi|^{-d+2s-4+\delta}\xi^{4}_{1},\quad\quad\,\,q_{2}(\xi)\coloneqq-i\dfrac{s-1}{\pi}c_{2}|\xi|^{-d+2s-4+\delta}\xi^{3}_{1},

\displaystyle q_{3}(\xi)\coloneqq i\dfrac{4\pi}{d+2s}c_{2}|\xi|^{-d+2s-2+\delta}\xi^{3}_{1},\quad\quad\quad\,\,\,q_{4}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{2d+4s+6}{d+2s}c_{2}|\xi|^{-d+2s-2+\delta}\xi^{2}_{1},

\displaystyle q_{5}(\xi)\coloneqq-i\dfrac{d+2s-1}{2\pi}c_{2}|\xi|^{-d+2s-2+\delta}\xi_{1},\quad q_{6}(\xi)\coloneqq i\dfrac{2\pi}{s(d+2s)}c_{2}|\xi|^{-d+2s+\delta}\xi_{1},

and denoting
c_{3}=\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)
,

\displaystyle q_{7}(\xi)\coloneqq-i\dfrac{4(s-1)}{d+2s}\pi c_{3}|\xi|^{-d+2s-2}\xi^{3}_{1},\,\,\,\,\quad\quad q_{8}(\xi)\coloneqq-(s-1)c_{3}|\xi|^{-d+2s-2}\xi^{2}_{1},

\displaystyle q_{9}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{d+2s}c_{3}|\xi|^{-d+2s}\xi^{2}_{1},\quad\quad\quad\,\,\,\,\,\quad\quad q_{10}(\xi)\coloneqq-i\dfrac{2d+4s+6}{d+2s}\pi c_{3}|\xi|^{-d+2s}\xi_{1},

\displaystyle q_{11}(\xi)\coloneqq-\dfrac{d+2s-1}{2}c_{3}|\xi|^{-d+2s},\quad\quad\quad\quad q_{12}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{s(d+2s)}c_{3}|\xi|^{-d+2s+2}.

Here, one can see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s-4+\delta}\xi^{4}_{1}\right](x)=\left(\frac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{4}\partial^{4}_{1}\left(\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s-4+\delta}\right](x)\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s+4-\delta}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s-4+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+4-\delta}{2}\right)}\partial^{4}_{1}(|x|^{-2s+4-\delta})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-\delta}}{4}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+4-\delta}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left[(2s+\delta)(2s+\delta+2)|x|^{-2s-\delta-4}x^{4}_{1}-6(2s+\delta)|x|^{-2s-\delta-2}x^{2}_{1}+3|x|^{-2s-\delta}\right],

so observing that the last one is a locally integrable function in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{1}](-e_{1})=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+4-\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{1-s}{d+2s}(2s+\delta-3)(2s+\delta-1).

We also calculate
\mathcal{F}[q_{2}](-e_{1})
,
\mathcal{F}[q_{3}](-e_{1})
, and
\mathcal{F}[q_{7}](-e_{1})
as follows, after observing that the corresponding expression is a locally integrable function in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s-4+\delta}\xi^{3}_{1}\right](x)

\displaystyle\quad=-i\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s+1-\delta}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+4-\delta}{2}\right)}\left((-2s-\delta)|x|^{-2s-\delta-2}x^{3}_{1}+3|x|^{-2s-\delta}x_{1}\right),

and so

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{2}](-e_{1})=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+4-\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{1-s}{2}\left(2s+\delta-3\right)

holds. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s-2+\delta}\xi^{3}_{1}\right](x)

\displaystyle\quad=-i\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-1-\delta}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+2+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}\left((-2s-\delta-2)|x|^{-2s-\delta-4}x^{3}_{1}+3|x|^{-2s-\delta-2}x_{1}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{-d+2s-2}\xi^{3}_{1}](x)\!=\!-i\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-1}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2}{2}\right)}\left((-2s\!-\!2)|x|^{-2s-4}x^{3}_{1}\!+\!3|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}\right)

so that we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{3}](-e_{1})=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{}{}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+2+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2\left(2s+\delta-1\right)}{d+2s}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{7}](-e_{1})=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2(1-s)(2s-1)}{d+2s}.

Now we start to compute
\mathcal{F}[q_{4}](-e_{1})
,
\mathcal{F}[q_{8}](-e_{1})
and
\mathcal{F}[q_{9}](-e_{1})
. Since

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{-d+2s-2+\delta}\xi_{1}^{2}]\!=\!\frac{1}{2}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-\delta}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}\left((-2s\!-\!\delta)|x|^{-2s-\delta-2}x_{1}^{2}\!+\!|x|^{-2s-\delta}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s-2}\xi_{1}^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2}{2}\right)}\left((-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}+|x|^{-2s}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s}\xi_{1}^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}\left((-2s-2)|x|^{-2s-4}x_{1}^{2}+|x|^{-2s-2}\right)

holds, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{4}](-e_{1})=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\left(2s+\delta-1\right)(d+2s+3)}{d+2s},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{8}](-e_{1})=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2}{2}\right)}\frac{(s-1)(1-2s)}{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{9}](-e_{1})=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2\left(2s+1\right)}{d+2s}.

Calculations of
\mathcal{F}[q_{5}](-e_{1})
,
\mathcal{F}[q_{6}](-e_{1})
and
\mathcal{F}[q_{10}](-e_{1})
are given as below. We see

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s-2+\delta}\xi_{1}\right]=-i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s+1-\delta}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}\left(|x|^{-2s-\delta}x_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s+\delta}\xi_{1}\right]=-i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-\delta-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s-\delta}{2}\right)}\left(|x|^{-2s-\delta-2}x_{1}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s}\xi_{1}\right]=-i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}\left(|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}\right),

so that we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{5}](-e_{1})=\dfrac{d+2s-1}{2}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{6}](-e_{1})=-\dfrac{2}{s(d+2s)}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s-\delta}{2}\right)},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{10}](-e_{1})=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2d+4s+6}{d+2s}.

Finally, we compute
\mathcal{F}[q_{11}](-e_{1})
and
\mathcal{F}[q_{12}](-e_{1})
in the following way. Since

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s}\right]=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}|x|^{-2s}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{-d+2s+2}\right]=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s-2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s-2}{2}\right)}|x|^{-2s-2},

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{11}](-e_{1})=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{d+2s-1}{2}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[q_{12}](-e_{1})=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s-2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2}{s(d+2s)}.

Note that
\mathcal{F}[q_{4}](x),\mathcal{F}[q_{5}](x),\dots,\mathcal{F}[q_{12}](x)
are locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
and so the above evaluations at
x=e_{1}
are justified.

Taking for the standard mollifier
\overline{\varphi}(x)
and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x-e_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{f\divideontimes g},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[q_{3}]\mathcal{F}[q_{4}]]},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[q_{3}]\mathcal{F}[q_{4}]](e_{1}),

where for the first equality we have used Corollary
B.16
and for the last equality we see that
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[q_{3}]\mathcal{F}[q_{4}]]
coincide to a function when
{\lvert x\rvert}>0
.

Therefore, we obtain
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
in case of
d\geq 3
as follows.

\displaystyle f_{2}(d,s,\delta)=(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[q_{3}\divideontimes q_{4}](-e_{1})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s+1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\left(\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2-\delta}{2}\right)}f_{2,1}(d,s,\delta)-\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2s+2}{2}\right)}f_{2,2}(d,s)\right),

where
f_{2,1}
is given in (
4.12
), and

\displaystyle f_{2,2}(d,s)

\displaystyle\!=\!-\dfrac{2s(1-s)(2s-1)}{d+2s}+\dfrac{(1-s)(2s-1)}{2}

\displaystyle\quad\!+\!\dfrac{s(2s+1)(d-2s)}{d+2s}-\dfrac{s(d+2s+3)(d-2s)}{d+2s}

\displaystyle\quad\!+\!\dfrac{(d+2s-1)(d-2s)}{4}-\dfrac{(d-2s-2)(d-2s)}{2(d+2s)}\!=\!\dfrac{(d-2)(d-1)(d-2s)}{4(d+2s)}.

Thus using
t\Gamma(t)=\Gamma(t+1)
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we obtain (
4.11
) in case of
d\geq 3
.

In case of
d=2
:
In this case, the computation of the term
f_{2,1}
is same, but
f_{2,2}
is different because of the log term. We will use the following: from

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{t}\xi_{1}^{k}\right]=\pi^{-t-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+t}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{t}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{k}\partial_{1}^{k}\left(|x|^{-d-t}\right)

for
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
t\notin\{0\}\cup 2\mathbb{N}
, by differentiating with respect to
t
in the distributional sense and using
\psi=\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}
, we have

(A.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{t}(\log|\xi|)\xi^{k}_{1}\right]\\
&\quad=-\pi^{-t-\frac{d}{2}}(\log\pi)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+t}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{t}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{k}\partial_{1}^{k}\left(|x|^{-d-t}\right)\\
&\quad\quad+\dfrac{1}{2}\pi^{-t-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+t}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{t}{2}\right)}\left(\psi\left(\frac{d+t}{2}\right)+\psi\left(-\frac{t}{2}\right)\right)\left(\frac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{k}\partial_{1}^{k}\left(|x|^{-d-t}\right)\\
&\quad\quad-\pi^{-t-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+t}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{t}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{k}\partial_{1}^{k}\left(|x|^{-d-t}\log|x|\right).\end{split}

Using the above equality together with Theorem
B.15
, we will compute

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(1-s)}{\Gamma\left(1+s\right)}\left(\log|\xi|+\gamma-\log 2\right)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\cdot\left(\dfrac{-2i(1-s)}{\pi(1+s)}|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{3}+\dfrac{s-1}{\pi^{2}}|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{2}-\dfrac{2}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\dfrac{(2s+5)i}{\pi(1+s)}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}\!+\!\dfrac{1\!+\!2s}{2\pi^{2}}|\xi|^{2s-2}\!-\!\dfrac{1}{s(1\!+\!s)}|\xi|^{2s}\right)\eqqcolon 2\pi^{2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(1\!-\!s)}{\Gamma\left(1\!+\!s\right)}\sum^{12}_{j=1}p_{j}(\xi),

where

\displaystyle p_{1}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{-2\pi i(1-s)}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-4}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}^{3},\quad p_{2}(\xi)\coloneqq(s-1)|\xi|^{2s-4}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle p_{3}(\xi)\coloneqq-\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-2}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}^{2},\quad\quad\quad\,\,p_{4}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{(2s+5)\pi i}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-2}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}

\displaystyle p_{5}(\xi)\coloneqq\dfrac{1+2s}{2}|\xi|^{2s-2}(\log|\xi|),\quad\quad\quad\quad\,\,\,p_{6}(\xi)\coloneqq-\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{s(1+s)}|\xi|^{2s}(\log|\xi|)

and with
c_{4}=\gamma-\log 2
,

\displaystyle p_{7}(\xi)\coloneqq-\pi c_{4}\dfrac{2i(1-s)}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{3},\quad\quad p_{8}(\xi)\coloneqq c_{4}(s-1)|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle p_{9}(\xi)\coloneqq-\pi^{2}c_{4}\dfrac{2}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}^{2},\quad\,\,\,\,\,\quad p_{10}(\xi)\coloneqq\pi c_{4}\dfrac{(2s+5)i}{1+s}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle p_{11}(\xi)\coloneqq c_{4}\dfrac{1+2s}{2}|\xi|^{2s-2},\quad\quad\quad\,\,\,\,\quad p_{12}(\xi)\coloneqq-\pi^{2}c_{4}\dfrac{1}{s(1+s)}|\xi|^{2s}.

For
p_{1}(\xi)
, use (
A.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{2s-4}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}^{3}]

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{8}\pi^{-2s}(\log\pi)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-s\right)}\partial_{1}^{3}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{16}\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(s-1)}{\Gamma(2-s)}(\psi(s-1)+\psi(2-s))\partial_{1}^{3}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{8}\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-s\right)}\partial_{1}^{3}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\log|x|\right).

Here, since
\partial_{1}^{3}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\right)=-8(s-1)s(s+1)|x|^{-2s-4}x_{1}^{3}+12(s-1)s|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}
and

\displaystyle\partial^{3}_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\log|x|\right)

\displaystyle=8(1-s^{2})s|x|^{-2s-4}(\log|x|)x_{1}^{3}+4(3s^{2}-1)|x|^{-2s-4}x_{1}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad-12(1-s)s|x|^{-2s-2}(\log|x|)x_{1}+6(1-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1},

observing that each expression is a locally integrable function in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{1}](-e_{1})

\displaystyle\!=\!-\dfrac{\pi^{-2s+1}}{2(1+s)}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(2\!-\!s)}\left\{(\log\pi)(s\!-\!1)2s(2s\!-\!1)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-(\psi(s\!-\!1)+\psi(2\!-\!s))(s\!-\!1)s(2s\!-\!1)\!-\!6s^{2}\!+\!6s\!-\!1\right\}

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{\pi^{-2s+1}}{2(1\!-\!s^{2})}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1\!-\!s)}\left\{(\log\pi)(s\!-\!1)2s(2s\!-\!1)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-(\psi(s)\!+\!\psi(1\!-\!s))(s\!-\!1)s(2s\!-\!1)\!-\!2s^{2}\!+\!4s\!-\!1\right\},

where for the last inequality we have used
\psi(s-1)+\psi(2-s)=\psi(s)+\psi(1-s)+\frac{2}{1-s}
.
For
p_{2}(\xi)
, use (
A.2
) to find

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{2s-4}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\pi^{-2s+1}(\log\pi)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-s\right)}\partial_{1}^{2}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{8}\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma(s-1)}{\Gamma(2-s)}(\psi(s-1)+\psi(2-s))\partial_{1}^{2}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-s\right)}\partial_{1}^{2}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\log|x|\right).

Here, one can use
\partial_{1}^{2}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\right)=(-2s+2)(-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}+(-2s+2)|x|^{-2s}
and

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s+2}\log|x|\right)

\displaystyle=-2s(2-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}\log|x|

\displaystyle\quad+2(1-s)|x|^{-2s}\log|x|+2(-2s+1)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}+|x|^{-2s}

to yield

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{2}](-e_{1})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{4}\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(2-s)}\left\{(\log\pi)(-2s+2)(-2s+1)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-(\psi(s-1)+\psi(2-s))(-2s+1)(1-s)-4s+3\right\}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi^{-2s+1}}{4(1-s)}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}\left\{(\log\pi)(-2s+2)(-2s+1)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))(-2s+1)(1-s)+1\right\},

where we have used
\psi(s-1)+\psi(2-s)=\psi(s)+\psi(1-s)+\frac{2}{1-s}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{3}](-e_{1})=\dfrac{-\pi^{-2s+1}}{2(1+s)}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1\!-\!s)}\left\{2(\log\pi)s(2s\!+\!1)\!-\!(\psi(s)\!+\!\psi(1\!-\!s))s(2s\!+\!1)\!-\!4s\!-\!1\right\}

holds. For
p_{4}(\xi)
, use (
A.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{2s-2}(\log|\xi|)\xi_{1}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2}\pi^{-2s}(\log\pi)\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-s\right)}\partial_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{4}\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))\partial_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{2}\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-s\right)}\partial_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s}\log|x|\right).

Here, since
\partial_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s}\right)=(-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}
and

\displaystyle\partial_{1}\left(|x|^{-2s}\log|x|\right)=(-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}\log|x|+|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}

holds, so we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{4}](-e_{1})=\dfrac{2s+5}{2(1+s)}\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}\left((\log\pi)2s-(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))s-1\right).

For the cases of
p_{5}(\xi)
and
p_{6}(\xi)
, the resulting calculations are as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{5}](-e_{1})=-\dfrac{1+2s}{2}\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}\left(\log\pi-\frac{1}{2}(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{6}](-e_{1})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{s(1+s)}\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma(1+s)}{\Gamma(-s)}\left(\log\pi-\frac{1}{2}(\psi(1+s)+\psi(-s))\right)

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{\pi^{-2s+1}}{1+s}\dfrac{\Gamma(1+s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}\left(\log\pi-\frac{1}{2}(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))-\frac{1}{s}\right).

Note that in the last equality we have used
\psi(1+s)+\psi(-s)=\psi(s)+\psi(1-s)+\frac{2}{s}
.
Now we start to compute
p_{7}(\xi)
. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi^{3}_{1}\right](x)

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{i}{8}\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(-s+2\right)}\partial^{3}_{1}(|x|^{-2s+2})

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{i}{2}\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(-s+2\right)}\left((-2-2s)|x|^{-2s-4}x_{1}^{3}+3|x|^{-2-2s}x_{1}\right),

and so

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{7}](-e_{1})=-(\gamma-\log 2)\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(1+s\right)}{\Gamma\left(-s+2\right)}\dfrac{(1-s)\left(2s-1\right)}{1+s}.

To obtain
p_{8}(\xi)
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi^{2}_{1}](x)\!=\!-\!\dfrac{\pi^{-2s+1}}{4}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\!-\!1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2\!-\!s\right)}\left((-2s\!+\!2)(-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}\!+\!(-2s\!+\!2)|x|^{-2s}\right)

holds, thus it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{8}](-e_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}(\gamma-\log 2)\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(2-s)}(s-1)(2s-1).

For
p_{9}(\xi)
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi^{2}_{1}](x)\!=\!-\dfrac{\pi^{-2s-1}}{4}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(1\!-\!s\right)}\left((-2s)(-2s\!-\!2)|x|^{-2s-4}x_{1}^{2}\!+\!(-2s)|x|^{-2s-2}\right)

holds, so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{9}](-e_{1})=(\gamma-\log 2)\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(s\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-s\right)}\dfrac{s(2s+1)}{1+s}.

The corresponding computation for the term
p_{10}(\xi)
is as follows. We use

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}\right](x)=\left(\dfrac{i}{2\pi}\right)\partial_{1}\left(\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{2s-2}\right](x)\right)=-i\pi^{-2s}\dfrac{s\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(-s+1)}|x|^{-2s-2}x_{1}

to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{10}](-e_{1})=-(\gamma-\log 2)\pi^{-2s+1}\dfrac{s\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}\dfrac{2s+5}{1+s}.

For
p_{11}(\xi)
,
\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{2s-2}\right](x)=\pi^{-2s+1}\frac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}|x|^{-2s}
and so

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{11}](-e_{1})=(\gamma-\log 2)\pi^{-2s+1}\frac{\Gamma(s)}{\Gamma(1-s)}\dfrac{1+2s}{2}.

For
p_{12}(\xi)
,
\mathcal{F}\left[|\xi|^{2s}\right](x)=\pi^{-2s-1}\frac{\Gamma(1+s)}{\Gamma(-s)}|x|^{-2-2s}
so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[p_{12}](-e_{1})=-(\gamma-\log 2)\pi^{-2s+1}\frac{\Gamma(1+s)}{\Gamma(-s)}\dfrac{1}{s(1+s)}.

Note that
\mathcal{F}[p_{2}](x),\mathcal{F}[p_{3}](x),\dots,\mathcal{F}[p_{12}](x)
are locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
and so the above evaluations at
x=e_{1}
are justified.

Taking for the standard mollifier
\overline{\varphi}(x)
and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x-e_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{f\divideontimes g},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]]},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]](e_{1}),

where for the first equality we have used Corollary
B.16
and for the last equality we see that
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]]
coincide to a function when
{\lvert x\rvert}>0
.

Summing up, we obtain
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
in case of
d=2
as follows:

(A.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{2}(d,s,\delta)=\dfrac{\pi}{2}\dfrac{\Gamma(1-s)}{\Gamma\left(1+s\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2s+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4-2s-\delta}{2}\right)}\tilde{f}_{2,1}(s,\delta)+\dfrac{\pi}{2s(1-s^{2})}\tilde{f}_{2,2}(s),\end{split}

where
\tilde{f}_{2,1}(s,\delta)\coloneqq f_{2,1}(2,s,\delta)
and

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{2,2}(s)

\displaystyle=\left[-(\log\pi)(s-1)2s(2s-1)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))(s-1)s(2s-1)+2s^{2}-4s+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{1}{2}(1+s)\left[(\log\pi)(-2s+2)(-2s+1)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))(-2s+1)(1-s)+1\right]

\displaystyle\quad-(1-s)\left[2(\log\pi)s(2s+1)-(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))s(2s+1)-4s-1\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(2s+5)(1-s)\left[2(\log\pi)s-(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))s-1\right]

\displaystyle\quad-(1-s^{2})(1+2s)\left[(\log\pi)-\frac{1}{2}(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))\right]

\displaystyle\quad-2(1-s)\left[(\log\pi)s-\dfrac{s}{2}(\psi(s)+\psi(1-s))-1\right]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}s-\tfrac{1}{2}.

Note that by the above computation together with the argument in the case
d\geq 3
, one can see that the expression (
A.3
) is the same as (
4.11
).
∎

Appendix B
Convolution theorems

This section provides the rigorous distributional framework and detailed Fourier analysis calculations justifying the results stated in Lemma
4.6
and Lemma
B.14
, where we compute the following terms
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,
f_{3}
, and
f_{4}
using Fourier transform. Throughout the section, we consider

\displaystyle d\geq 2\quad\text{with}\quad d\in\mathbb{N},\quad s\in(0,1),

and
\delta\in[0,\tfrac{1}{2}]
for Subsection
B.1
and Subsection
B.2
, and
\delta\in[0,\tfrac{d}{2})
for Subsection
B.3
and Subsection
B.4
. We define

(B.1)

\displaystyle f_{1}(d,s,\delta)\coloneqq\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{|e_{1}+h|^{1-\delta}\widehat{1+h_{1}}-1}{|h|^{d+2s}}\,dh,

(B.2)

\displaystyle f_{2}(d,s,\delta)

\displaystyle\quad=\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left<\dfrac{\widehat{e_{1}+h}\otimes\widehat{e_{1}+h}+e_{1}\otimes e_{1}}{2}\widehat{h},\widehat{h}\right>\dfrac{|e_{1}+h|^{1-\delta}\widehat{1+h_{1}}-1}{|h|^{d+2s}}\,dh

\displaystyle\quad=\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{-2h_{1}^{3}\!+\!|h|^{2}h_{1}^{2}\!+\!2h_{1}^{2}\!-\!2|h|^{2}h_{1}\!+\!|h|^{4}}{2|h|^{d+2s+2}|e_{1}-h|^{2}}\left(|e_{1}\!-\!h|^{-\delta}(1\!-\!h_{1})\!-\!1\right)dh,

(B.3)

\displaystyle f_{3}(d,s,\delta):=\text{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{1-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh,

(B.4)

\displaystyle f_{4}(d,s,\delta):=\text{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-1-\delta}\widehat{1-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh.

Let us explain the notation. By p.v. we denote the
principal value
of an integral, where we define the principal value as the limit of the integrals over
B_{\frac{1}{r}}(0)\setminus B_{r}(0)
for
r\rightarrow 0
. The unit vector
(1,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is denoted by
e_{1}
. We use
\widehat{x}=\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}
and
\widehat{x_{1}}=\frac{x_{1}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}
for vectors
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
x\otimes x
denotes the
d\times d
matrix given by
(x\otimes x)_{i,j}=x_{i}x_{j}
for any
1\leq i,j\leq d
.

Let us mention that for the last equality of (
B.2
) uses elementary manipulations only. The integrals in the definition of (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) are finite due to Lemma
3.1
. The integrals in the definition of (
B.3
) and (
B.4
) are finite due to Lemma
B.20
and
B.30
, respectively.

The main results of this note are Theorem
B.7
, Theorem
B.15
, Theorem
B.25
, and Theorem
B.35
together with corresponding corollaries. The main results represent the values
f_{i}
as certain convolutions of two distributions evaluated at
x=e_{1}
. Let us provide details for the case of
f_{1}
. For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define distributions
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
by

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\Delta\varphi(x)}{2s(d+2s-2)}\,dx.

We will show in Lemma
B.4

f_{1}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
, where
\varphi_{\varepsilon}
is an approximation of the identity at
e_{1}
and the convolution
g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}
is defined as in
[
Vla02
]
. The evaluation then hinges on Theorem
B.7
where, if
\delta>0
, we show

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]

in the distributional sense. Here,
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
denotes the usual multiplication of
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
.

The paper is organized as follows. Each of the subsequent sections provides detailed calculations
f_{1}(d,s,\delta)
,
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
,
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)
and
f_{4}(d,s,\delta)
respectively. The strategy that we employ each subsection is as follows:

(a)

First we give definitions of distributions associated to
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,
f_{3}
, and
f_{4}
.

(See Definition
B.1
, Definition
B.9
, Definition
B.17
, and Definition
B.27
.)

(b)

Then we prove the well-definedness of their convolutions in a distributional sense.

(See Lemma
B.2
with Lemma
B.3
, Lemma
B.10
with Lemma
B.11
, Lemma
B.18
with Lemma
B.19
, and Lemma
B.28
with Lemma
B.29
.)

(c)

Furthermore, we show that these convolutions recover
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,
f_{3}
, and
f_{4}
when tested against an approximation to identity centered at
x=e_{1}
.

(See Lemma
B.4
, Lemma
B.12
, Lemma
B.22
, and Lemma
B.32
.)

(d)

By (b), we can apply convolution theorem as in
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
. Moreover, with our specific distributions in Definition
B.1
, Definition
B.9
, Definition
B.17
, and Definition
B.27
, the resulting product of the Fourier transforms of the two distributions can be understood in a certain way.

(See Theorem
B.7
, Theorem
B.15
, Theorem
B.25
, and Theorem
B.35
.)

B.1.
Convolution theorem for
f_{1}(d,s,\delta)

We define the distributions
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
, where (formal) evaluation
x=e_{1}
to their convolution will recover
f_{1}(d,s,\delta)
.

Definition B.1
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define distributions
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}\widehat{x}_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx,

and

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\Delta\varphi(x)}{2s(d+2s-2)}\,dx.

We observe the following:

•

One can easily check that the above distributions are well-defined since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
{\lvert\partial^{k}_{i}\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d+2}}\}
for
k=0,1,2
and
i=0,1,\dots,d
.

•

The distribution
g_{1}
can be understood as a locally integrable function in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
since
\delta\in\left[0,\tfrac{1}{2}\right]
.

•

When
{\lvert x\rvert}>0
,
g_{2}(x)\equiv{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}
holds.

The main goal of this subsection is to show the following relation (see Theorem
B.7
): For
\delta>0
,

(B.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]

in the distributional sense, where
\divideontimes
is a convolution of two distributions in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
introduced in
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, and
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
. The well-definedness of the left-hand side term of (
B.5
) will be proved in Lemma
B.2
and Lemma
B.3
, and the well-definedness of the right-hand side term of (
B.5
) will be proved in Lemma
B.5
.

The case
\delta=0
is excluded, because (
B.5
) does not hold in this case. An additional term involving distributions would appear. However, for the computation of
f_{1}(d,s,0)
, the case
\delta=0
is not needed as
f_{1}(d,s,0)=0
from (
B.1
). Note that in Lemma
B.2
–Lemma
B.4
the case of
\delta=0
is included, but in Lemma
B.5
and the remaining part of this subsection we assume
\delta>0
.

From now on, let
c
be a generic constant, and we use brackets to clarify the dependence of
c
, e.g.,
c=c(d,s,\delta)
means that
c
depends only on
d,s
, and
\delta
. For numbers or functions
a
and
b
, we occasionally use
a\lesssim b
, if there exists an implicit constant
c>0
such that
a\leq cb
.

First, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.2
.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}
is well-defined, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

\displaystyle\quad=\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

exists for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.

Let us choose
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
R\geq 2
. Before starting the proof, let us explain the basic strategy. In the above integral, the term
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}
is integrable near 0, which means

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy<\infty,

and it is not integrable near infinity, which means

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy=\infty.

Thus together with the fact that
y\mapsto{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}
is even, we want to estimate as follows to estimate
{\lvert{\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}\rvert}<\infty
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}(x+y)\,dx\leq c\,R^{d}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+\bigg(c{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.\end{split}

(See (
B.9
) below.)

Let us first estimate the following term:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx.

Note that for each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. When
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
,
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
implies
-\delta>-d
, so we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq\int_{B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\lesssim R^{d-\delta+1}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx,

where for the last inequality we have used
{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim 1
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Here and throughout the proof of this lemma, implicit generic constants
c
in ‘
\lesssim
’ depend only on

\displaystyle d,s,\delta,\quad\text{and}\quad{\lVert\nabla^{i}\varphi\rVert}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1,2.

Then if
-y\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
, then using
{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-2}
(since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-2}\,dx\lesssim R^{-\delta-1}.

On the other hand, if
-y\not\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
so that
B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)=\emptyset
, then employing
{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-2}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)\cup B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y))}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim R^{-\delta+1}\cdot R^{d}+R^{-\delta+1}R^{-2}\lesssim R^{d+1}

holds, since
R\geq 2
. Then if
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

(B.6)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d+1}.

Now we consider the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
. In this case we will use the fact that the integral of the odd function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is zero as well as the divergence theorem. Using
\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)=\Delta_{x}\varphi(x+y)
, observe that

(B.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\\
&\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\Delta\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\Delta\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\Delta\varphi(x)\,dx\end{split}

holds. Here, using divergence theorem, we obtain

(B.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\Delta\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\Delta_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad\quad+\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad\quad-\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\nabla_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))\cdot\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\,dx.\end{split}

Using
\Delta_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))=-(d-\delta)\delta{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\Delta_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=-(d-\delta)\delta\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx.

Moreover,

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}{\lvert x\rvert}\quad\text{for}\quad x\in B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)

from Lemma
2.5
and
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2d}\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}\varphi(x)\,dx\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}.

Thus, merging the above two estimates, we find that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\Delta_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq(d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx+c{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}.

Also, by
{\lvert\nabla\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}\}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2}
on
x\in\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta+1-d-2}{\lvert y\rvert}^{d-1}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}.

Finally, using
{\lvert\nabla_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta}
when
x\in\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)
and
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\leq\min\{1,{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}\}
,

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\nabla_{x}({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}))\cdot\nu\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{d-1}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}

holds. Then for (
B.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\Delta\varphi(x)\,dx\leq(d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx+c{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}.

Summing up, with (
B.6
) we see that

(B.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}(x+y)\,dx\\
&\quad\leq c\,R^{d+1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+\bigg((d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.\end{split}

Note that the integral of
\varphi(\cdot)
over
B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)
is bounded since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

For
L\geq 2R
, using (
B.9
),
\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)=\Delta_{x}\varphi(x+y)
, and
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}>0
, we estimate as follows:

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R}R^{d+1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\bigg((d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}dy.

Here, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R}R^{d+1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\lesssim R^{d-2s+3}.

Moreover, using Fubini’s theorem and observing that
y\mapsto{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}y_{1}
is odd, we find

(B.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg){\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}dy\\
&\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\int_{\max\{2{\lvert x\rvert},R\}\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2-\delta-2}y_{1}\,dy\bigg)\varphi(x)dx=0.\end{split}

Also, we see that

(B.11)

\displaystyle\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}dy\lesssim R^{-2s}.

Merging the above three estimates, it follows that

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim R^{d-2s+3}+R^{-2s}\lesssim R^{d+3}.

Using the above argument, we can obtain that for
\widetilde{L}>L
, we have

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq c{\bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\bigg((d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}dy\bigg\rvert}.

By Fubini’s theorem and observing that
y\mapsto{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}y_{1}
is odd, we find

(B.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg){\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}dy\\
&\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\int_{\max\{2{\lvert x\rvert},L\}\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2-\delta-2}y_{1}\,dy\bigg)\varphi(x)dx=0.\end{split}

Also, we see that

(B.13)

\displaystyle\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}dy\lesssim L^{-2s}.

Merging the above three estimates, it follows that

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim L^{-2s}.

This proves that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

is a Cauchy sequence for
L
. Then we see that

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

\displaystyle\quad=\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

exists. Therefore, we get the conclusion.
∎

Following
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, let
\eta\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be such that

(B.14)

\eta(x)=1
in
x\in B_{1}(0)
,
\operatorname{supp}\eta\subset B_{2}(0)
, and
{\lvert\nabla^{i}\eta\rvert}\leq c(d)
for
i=0,1,2,3
.

Define
\eta_{k}(x)=\eta(x/k)
as an 1-sequence in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Also, with
[
Vla02
, Page 52]
, let
\xi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
be such that

(B.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{$\xi(x,y)=1$ in $(x,y)\in B_{1}(0)\times B_{1}(0)$, $\operatorname{supp}\xi\subset B_{2}(0)\times B_{2}(0)$,}\\
&\text{and ${\lvert\nabla^{i}\xi\rvert}\leq c(d)$ for any $i=0,1,2,3$.}\end{split}

Define
\xi_{j}(x,y)=\xi(x/j,y/j)
as an 1-sequence in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
. Now we prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.3
.

The convolution of distributions
g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

(B.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}&={\langle{g_{2}\divideontimes g_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}\\
&:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{2})(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.\end{split}

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}={\big\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.

Proof.

Note that once the double limit in the right-hand side of (
B.16
) exists, then

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}
