\displaystyle:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{2})(\cdot)\times g_{1}(\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.28671pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.1909pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}})},{\xi_{j}(\cdot,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.28671pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.1909pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}})\varphi(\cdot+\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.28671pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.1909pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}})}\rangle}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{2})(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}

holds. Here, for the first definition since
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset(C^{\infty}_{c})^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\xi_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
,
[
Vla02
, Page 96 and Page 52]
is used, and for the second definition since
\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
,
[
Vla02
, Page 41]
is used. Moreover, by
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, we have
{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}={\langle{g_{2}\divideontimes g_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}
.

Now it suffices to show that

(B.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{2})(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}\\
&\quad={\big\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.\end{split}

To do this, for
k,j>1
, we have

\displaystyle{\big\lvert{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{2})(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}-{\big\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\big\lvert{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{2})(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{(1-\xi_{j}(x,y))\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\big\lvert{\big\langle{(1-\eta_{k})g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

Using
\sum^{3}_{i=0}{\lvert\nabla^{i}[(1-\xi^{j}(x,y))\varphi(x+y)]\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
{\lvert\nabla^{i}\xi\rvert}\leq c(d)
for
i=0,1,2,3
,
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2k
and
{\lvert y\rvert}>j
with
j\geq 4k
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2k}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}>j}{\lvert\eta_{k}(x)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}({\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{d+1-\delta}j^{-d-2s+2}\underset{j\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

For
I_{2}
, we write the integrand
J(x,y):\mathbb{R}^{2d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\big\langle{(1-\eta_{k})g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}=:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J(x,y)\,dy\,dx=\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}>k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J(x,y)\,dx\,dy,

where for the last equality we have used
\eta_{k}(\cdot)\equiv 1
when
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq k
. Then following the proof of Lemma
B.2
, we obtain (
B.9
) with the choice of
x
replaced by
y
and
R=k/2
. Then imitating the remaining
proof of Lemma
B.2
but only considering the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
in its proof, we get the analogous estimate of (
B.11
) as follows:

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}>k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J(x,y)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim k^{-2s}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Then (
B.17
) holds and we conclude the proof.
∎

Now we define the standard mollifier
\overline{\varphi}(x)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as

(B.18)

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}(x)=\begin{cases}\overline{c}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1-{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\right)&\quad\text{if }{\lvert x\rvert}<1\\
0&\quad\text{if }{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1,\end{cases}

where
\overline{c}
is defined with the following property
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\overline{\varphi}(x)\,dx=1
.
Also, we denote

\displaystyle\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x-e_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)

for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then we prove the following lemma, which rigorously establishes the connection between
f_{1}(d,s,\delta)
in (
B.2
) and
g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}
.

Lemma B.4
.

We have

f_{1}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
.

Proof.

With
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
, we observe that

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{g_{1}(x)},{{\langle{g_{2}(y)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\rangle}_{x}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy.

Before starting the proof, we explain the strategy. We want to apply the argument in the proof of
[
Eva10
, Page 714, Theorem 7]
. But since
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are distributions, and moreover, for
g_{2}
the integrand
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}
in Definition
B.1
is different from the expression of
g_{2}\equiv{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}
when
{\lvert x\rvert}>0
. To overcome this issue, we first use divergence theorem to send the Laplacian
\Delta
applied for the test function
\varphi(x)
to
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}
, to recover the expression
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}
(See
Step 1
). Then we use the argument of
[
Eva10
, Page 714, Theorem 7]
(See
Step 2
).

We divide the proof into two steps.

Step 1: Modifying the formula of
{\langle{g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
using divergence theorem.

In this step we will show that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

holds. For
\sigma\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
with
\sigma\leq\varepsilon
, and a cross-shaped area

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}:=(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cup(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}),

we will first show

(B.19)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\iint_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy=0.

To this end, we observe that

(B.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\Big\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\\
&\quad\leq\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}\,dx\,dy\end{split}

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=(\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\cup(\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}),

where for the last inequality we have used the fact that since
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, on the set
(x,y)\in\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}
we obtain
\operatorname{supp}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\subset\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}
.
Then we argue as follows:

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\iint_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+c(d,s,\varepsilon)\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)(\sigma^{d-\delta+1}+\sigma^{2-2s})\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{2-2s}.

Here, the
\varepsilon
dependence to the universal constant is from the term
{\lvert\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}
. Thus for each fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
, for any
\sigma\in(0,\varepsilon]
we have found the above estimate. Now sending
\sigma\rightarrow 0
, together with (
B.20
), we obtain (
B.19
).

Now for
\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}=(\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1/\sigma\})\cup(\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq 2/\sigma\})
, we have to show

(B.21)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\iint_{\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy=0.

To do this, note that on the set
\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1/\sigma\}
, we have
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=0
from
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1/\sigma\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dy\,dx.

Now by following the proof of Lemma
B.2
, as in (
B.9
) with the choice of
R=\frac{1}{\sigma}
, when
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\leq(d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\Delta\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx+\frac{c(d,s,\varepsilon)}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\end{split}

holds. Then following the remaining proof of Lemma
B.2
, especially from (
B.10
) and (
B.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{2s}.

On the set
\{x:{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{y:{\lvert y\rvert}>2/\sigma\}
, we have
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=0
from
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq 2/\sigma\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dy\,dx.

Now by following the proof of Lemma
B.2
, as in (
B.9
) with the choice of
R=\frac{2}{\sigma}
, when
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{2}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\leq(d-\delta)\delta{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx+\frac{c(d,s,\varepsilon)}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\end{split}

holds. Then following the remaining proof of Lemma
B.2
, especially from (
B.10
) and (
B.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\int_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}>2/\sigma\}}\int_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{2s}.

For the integral on the set
\{y:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{x:{\lvert x\rvert}>1/\sigma\}
is similar so we get

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\int_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}}\int_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1/\sigma\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{2s}.

Thus for each fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
, for any
\sigma\in(0,\varepsilon]
we have found the above estimate. Now sending
\sigma\rightarrow 0
establishes (
B.21
).

We are now ready to apply divergence theorem. Using

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=\Delta_{y}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)),

and
\nabla_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)
, for

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}\times\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 2/\sigma\},

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\left(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dS

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2s}\int_{\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}\left(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dS

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}(\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma})+J_{3}(\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}).

Here,
\nu(x,y)
is the outward pointing unit normal vector, and

\displaystyle\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 2/\sigma\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert x\rvert}=1/\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 2/\sigma\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}=2/\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}

\displaystyle:=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

On
I_{1}
, using
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\equiv 0
on
{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma
,
{\lvert\nu(x,y)\rvert}=1
, and
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert J_{2}(I_{1})\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{1}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{I_{1}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}\,dS

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}\,dS

\displaystyle\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{d-\delta}

and

\displaystyle{\lvert J_{3}(I_{1})\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}\,dS

\displaystyle\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{d-\delta}.

For the integral on the set
I_{2}
, using divergence theorem again, together with
\nu(x,y)=\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}
on
I_{2}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}\left({\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\frac{x}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\right)=(d-\delta){\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1},

and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\equiv 0
on
{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}
, we write

\displaystyle-2s(d+2s-2)

\displaystyle J_{2}(I_{2})=\int_{I_{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dS

\displaystyle=-(-d-2s+2)(d-\delta)\int_{I_{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dS.

Here, we have used the fact that on

\displaystyle\partial(I_{2})=\{{\lvert x\rvert}=1/\sigma,{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma\}\cup\{{\lvert x\rvert}=1/\sigma,{\lvert y\rvert}=2/\sigma\},

since
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\equiv 0
on
\partial(I_{2})
. Then we have

(B.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{I_{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dS\\
&=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma}}{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})\,dS.\end{split}

Now, since
x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}
is odd and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})=\varphi_{\varepsilon}(-y+e_{1})
, note that

(B.23)

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})\,dS=0.

Then for
{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma}\leq 2{\lvert x\rvert}
, using

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}

by Lemma
2.5
and using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})\subset B_{1}(e_{1})
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma}}{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})\,dS

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})\,dS\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-e_{1})\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma^{-d+1}\sigma^{d+2s-1}\sigma^{\delta}\lesssim\sigma^{2s+\delta}.

Thus, we have found

\displaystyle J_{2}(I_{2})\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{2s+\delta}.

Also, for
J_{3}
on
I_{2}
, similar to (
B.22
), using (
B.23
) and

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert y-x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-d-2s}(y-x-e_{1})+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}x\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert},

together with
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\lvert J_{3}(I_{2})\rvert}\lesssim{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{2}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dS\bigg\rvert}\leq c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{2s+\delta}.

For the integral on the set
I_{3}
, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
,
\sigma\leq\varepsilon
, and the fact that
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\lvert J_{2}(I_{3})\rvert}

\displaystyle={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim c(d,s,\varepsilon)\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+3}\,dS\lesssim\sigma^{d-1}\sigma^{-d-2s+3}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

Also, for
J_{3}
, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, and then
{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}
and
\nu(y)=-\frac{y}{{\lvert y\rvert}}
, we estimate

\displaystyle{\lvert J_{3}(I_{3})\rvert}\lesssim{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim c(d,s,\varepsilon)\sigma^{-d-2s+3}\sigma^{d-1}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

Finally, we consider the integral on
I_{4}
. Using
\nu(x,y)=\frac{y}{{\lvert y\rvert}}
and divergence theorem, together with

(B.24)

\displaystyle\nabla({\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1})=-\delta{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}x+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1},

and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=0
on
I_{4}
, we have

\displaystyle-2s(d+2s-2)J_{2}(I_{4})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{2}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dS

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{2}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}y(-\delta{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}x+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1})\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dS.

Here, we have used the fact that on

\displaystyle\partial(I_{4})=\{{\lvert y\rvert}=2/\sigma,{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma\}\cup\{{\lvert y\rvert}=2/\sigma,{\lvert x\rvert}=1/\sigma\},

since
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\equiv 0
on
\partial(I_{4})
. Then we have

(B.25)

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{2}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}y(-\delta{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}x+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1})\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dS=0,

since

(B.26)

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{2}{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}y\,dS=0.

Also, for
J_{3}
, recalling
\nu(x,y)=\frac{y}{{\lvert y\rvert}}
, and using (
B.26
),
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=0
on
I_{4}
, and
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, we observe

\displaystyle{\lvert J_{3}(I_{4})\rvert}

\displaystyle={\bigg\lvert\frac{1}{2s}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{2}{\sigma}}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)){\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\nu(x,y)\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2s}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{2}{\sigma}}\int_{1-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x){\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta+1}\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}\,dS\lesssim\sigma^{d+2s-1}\sigma^{-d+1}\lesssim\sigma^{2s}.

Then similar to (
B.25
), we obtain
J_{3}\lesssim\sigma^{2s}
in this case.

Overall, recalling
\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}
,
\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2s(d+2s-2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}G_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\left(\int_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}G_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy+\int_{\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}}G_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\left(\int_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy,

where
G_{\varepsilon}(x,y)={\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\Delta_{y}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)
.

Step 2: Proof of (
B.47
).
We need to show

(B.27)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy=f_{1}(d,s,\delta).

To do this, for fixed
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
, consider
\varepsilon\in(0,1/100)
with
2\varepsilon\leq\kappa
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}=\mathbb{R}^{2d}\setminus(\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}\cup\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}).

We first estimate the integration on
\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}
. Using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
and the fact that
y\mapsto{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y
is odd, and for
q(y)={\lvert x+e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}+1-y_{1})
when
x\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
and
y\in B_{\kappa}(0)
,

\displaystyle{\lvert q(y)-q(0)-y\cdot\nabla q(0)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}

holds, so using change of variables, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\frac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}\left(q(y)-q(0)-y\cdot\nabla q(0)\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.

Now we consider the integral on the set
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}
. Let us write

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy-\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=:K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3}.

For
K_{1}
, we compute

\displaystyle K_{1}=\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}\hskip-17.07164pt\int_{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\hskip-17.07164pt\left({\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,+\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert},\varepsilon\}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}^{1-\delta}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{d+1-\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{d},

where for the first inequality we have used that

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx=0

as well as that on the set
\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon
, by Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert},

and on the set
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert},\varepsilon\}
,
{\lvert{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}^{1-\delta}
holds.

Also, for
K_{2}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\leq\kappa^{d},

and for
K_{3}
, by divergence theorem, using (
B.24
) we have

\displaystyle K_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\left(-\delta{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}x+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\left(-\delta{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}x+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{d}.

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\\
\cup{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{d}.

Now on the set
\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}
, the integrand
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))
is no longer singular, so we can apply the argument of the proof of
[
Eva10
, Page 714, Theorem 7]
. In detail, let us write

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy-\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=:L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3}.

For
L_{1}
, recalling
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
and
\operatorname{supp}\overline{\varphi}\subset B_{1}(0)
, with
[
Eva10
, Page 715]
we obtain

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\,\,-\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(y_{1}+1)\,dy.

For
L_{2}
, we estimate

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}(x_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)dx\,dy\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}\,dy.

Finally, for
L_{3}
, by divergence theorem,

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle=-\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=-\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\cdot\left(-\delta{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}x+{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=-\frac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\cdot\left(-\delta{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-1)(x-e_{1})+{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta}e_{1}\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\,\,(\delta-1)\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dy.

Then note that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}L_{3}=(\delta-1)\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert e_{1}+y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dy+(\delta-1)\int_{{\lvert e_{1}+y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{d},

since the first term in the right-hand side is zero by symmetry and
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y
is odd.

Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\hskip-14.22636pt{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))dy-L_{1}-L_{2}\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}

together with

\displaystyle\lim_{\kappa\rightarrow 0}L_{1}+L_{2}=f_{1}(d,s,\delta).

Since
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
is arbitrary chosen and
\varepsilon
is already sent to zero. The proof is complete.
∎

For the remaining part of this subsection, we assume
\delta>0
. We have the following.

Lemma B.5
.

For
\delta>0
, we have

(B.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}[g_{1}](\xi)=-\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}+\delta-1}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}\xi_{1}:=\mathcal{G}_{1}(\xi)\\
&\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}[g_{2}](\xi)=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{2s}:=\mathcal{G}_{2}(\xi)\end{split}

in the distributional sense.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{1}](\xi)=\dfrac{1}{2-\delta}\mathcal{F}[\partial_{1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2-\delta}]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2\pi i\xi_{1}}{2-\delta}\mathcal{F}[{\lvert x\rvert}^{2-\delta}]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2\pi i\xi_{1}}{2-\delta}\dfrac{(2\pi)^{\delta-2}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}2^{\delta-2}\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta-2}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}

\displaystyle=-\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}+\delta-1}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}\xi_{1}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{2}](\xi)=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{2s}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Now with
\phi_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
where
\overline{\varphi}
is a standard mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma B.6
.

For
\delta>0
, the multiplication of distributions
\mathcal{G}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{2}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{2}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}

exists. Moreover, we have

(B.29)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{2}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathcal{G}_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathcal{G}_{2}=\pi^{\delta-1+2s}i\dfrac{\Gamma(-s)\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta+2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2+2s}\xi_{1}

means the usual multiplication.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be given. We will show that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\lvert{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{2}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}-{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathcal{G}_{2}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\rangle}\big\rvert}=0.

For the proof, we use Lebesgue differentiation theorem. For
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon>0
, consider

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}x_{1}\right]\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2+2s}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad=:{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}+{\lvert I_{2}\rvert}.

Using the fact that
x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2+2s}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}
is odd, and
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, note that

(B.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}&={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2+2s}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx\bigg\rvert}\\
&=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta+2s}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}.\end{split}

For
I_{2}
, using the fact that the map

\displaystyle x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\dfrac{\varphi(x)+\varphi(-x)}{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)

is odd since
\phi_{\varepsilon}
is even, we write

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx.

Let us estimate the first term in the right-hand side. We have

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx,

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\,dy=0.

Using
{\lvert\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1-d}{\lvert y\cdot\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rvert}
, we further compute

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s+1}\,dx\lesssim\varepsilon^{2s+\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{2s+\delta},

where we have used
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
. For the other term, using
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s+1}\varepsilon^{\delta-1}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\right)\,dx\lesssim\varepsilon^{2s+\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{2s+\delta}.

Note that the implicit constants in the above estimates do not depend on
\varepsilon
. Also, using
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
and
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{{\lvert x-y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\overline{c}\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\overline{c}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta+2s}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}.

Then merging the estimates, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{1}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2s+\delta}.

Now similar to the proof of Lemma
B.4
, since on the set
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa
with
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
, both
\mathcal{G}_{1}(x)
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)
are no longer singular, by Lebesgue differentiation theorem we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{1}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}=0.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\lvert{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{2}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}-{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathcal{G}_{2}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\rangle}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{1}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{1}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2s+\delta}.

Since
\kappa
was arbitrary, we obtain (
B.29
).
∎

With the help of Lemma
B.5
and
B.6
, we have the following theorem.

Theorem B.7
.

For
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]

in the distributional sense, where
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
.

Proof.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, note that

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{(\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}](y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{F}[g_{2}](x)},{\varphi(x)}\big\rangle}_{x}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]},{\varphi}\rangle},

where for the first equality since
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have used
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
together with Lemma
B.2
and Lemma
B.3
, for the second equality since
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
as in
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
, where with multi index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{d})
,

(B.31)

\displaystyle\theta_{M}:=\{f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):{\lvert\partial^{\alpha}f(x)\rvert}\leq c_{\alpha}(1+{\lvert x\rvert})^{m_{\alpha}}\quad\text{for some }c_{\alpha},m_{\alpha}>0\}

(defined in
[
Vla02
, Page 76]
) and
\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
(\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
holds as in
[
Vla02
, Page 79]
, for the third equality again since
\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
,
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
is used, and for the last equality Lemma
B.6
together with Lemma
B.5
is used.
∎

Then it is worth mentioning the following corollary.

Corollary B.8
.

For
\delta>0
, we have
(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}]=\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]\right]
.

Proof.

Since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by Theorem
B.7
we have

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{1}]\mathcal{F}[g_{2}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle},

which proves the conclusion.
∎

B.2.
Convolution theorem for
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)

We define the distributions
g_{3}
and
g_{4}
, where the (formal) evaluation
x=e_{1}
to their convolution will recover
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
. The definition is the same as Definition
A.1
.

Definition B.9
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define the distributions
g_{3}
and
g_{4}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle g_{3}:=j_{1}-j_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad g_{4}:=-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2},

where the distributions
j_{1},j_{2},\dots,j_{7}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
are defined as

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}h_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\begin{cases}-\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx&\,\text{if }d=2,\\
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx&\,\text{if }d\geq 3,\end{cases}

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{3}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}h_{1}^{3}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)x_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\partial_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s}h_{1}^{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}x_{1}^{2}\varphi(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{5}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}h_{1}^{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{2s{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\partial_{1}^{2}\varphi(x)-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\Delta\varphi(x)}{2s(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\,dx,

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s}h_{1}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\partial_{1}\varphi(x)}{-d-2s+2}\,dx,

and

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{7}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\varphi(x)\,dx.

Note that

•

One can easily check that the above distributions are well-defined since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
{\lvert\partial^{k}_{i}\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{3}}\}
for
k=0,1,2,3
and
i=0,1,\dots,d
.

•

Distributions
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
when
d\geq 3
,
j_{4}
, and
j_{7}
can be understood as locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, since (especially for
j_{1}
)
\delta\in\left[0,\tfrac{1}{2}\right]
.

•

Also, when
{\lvert x\rvert}>0
,
j_{2}(x)\equiv-\operatorname{div}\left(\frac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\right)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}
holds in case of
d=2
,
j_{3}(x)\equiv{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}x_{1}^{3}
,
j_{5}(x)\equiv{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}
, and
j_{6}(x)\equiv{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}x_{1}
.

•

Note that when
d=2
, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and any
\kappa\in(0,\infty)
, by divergence theorem we have

(B.32)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle{j_{2}},{\varphi}\rangle}&=-\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla(\varphi(x)-\varphi(0))\,dx-\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&=\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(0)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx+\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\dfrac{\varphi(x)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx+{\lvert S^{1}\rvert}\log(\kappa)\varphi(0),\end{split}

where
{\lvert S^{1}\rvert}
means the circumference of the
2
-dimensional unit sphere.

The main goal of this subsection is to show the following relation (see Theorem
B.15
):

(B.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]

in the distributional sense, where
\divideontimes
is a convolution of two distributions in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
introduced in
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, and
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
. Note that from Lemma
A.2
,\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
are locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The well-definedness of the left-hand side term of (
B.33
) will be proved in Lemma
B.10
and Lemma
B.11
, and the well-definedness of the right-hand side term of (
B.33
) will be proved in Lemma
5.4
.

To show (
B.33
), first we prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.10
.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\langle{g_{4}(y)},{{\langle{g_{3}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}
is well-defined.

Proof.

Let us choose
R\geq 2
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and then divide the proof into three steps. Recall that
g_{3}:=j_{1}-j_{2}
and
g_{4}:=-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2}
. Here and throughout the proof of this lemma, implicit generic constants
c
in ‘
\lesssim
’ will depend only on

\displaystyle d,s,\delta,\quad\text{and}\quad{\lVert\nabla^{i}\varphi\rVert}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1,2,3.

Note that for each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the integrand in defining
{\langle{g_{3}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}
is in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Step 1: Estimate for
j_{2}
in the case of
d=2
.
When
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq{\Bigg\lvert\int_{B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}+{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim R\log R+{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert},

where for the last inequality we have used
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\rvert}\lesssim 1
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Here, if
-y\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
, then employing
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-3}
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\frac{1}{{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{3}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim\frac{\log R}{R^{2}}

holds. On the other hand, if
-y\not\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
so that
B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)=\emptyset
, then using
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-3}\}
,

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)}\dfrac{\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)\cup B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y))}\dfrac{\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{3}}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim R\log R+\dfrac{\log R}{R}\cdot\frac{1}{R}\lesssim R\log R

holds, since
R\geq 2
. Then if
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

(B.34)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R\log R.

Now we consider the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
. Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx+\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{(x\cdot\nu(x,y))\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\varphi(x+y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{((x-y)\cdot x)\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\varphi(x)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{(x-y)\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx,

where for the second inequality we have used divergence theorem with the term
-\operatorname{div}\left(\frac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\right)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}
for
x\neq 0
, and
\nu(x,y)=\widehat{x+y}
means the outward pointing unit normal vector along
\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)
. Here, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1/{\lvert x\rvert}^{2-s}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and recalling
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\varphi(x)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2-s}}\,dx\lesssim\dfrac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{s}\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}.

Moreover, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1/{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and employing
\log{\lvert y\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s}
,

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{((x-y)\cdot x)\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\varphi(x)\,dS\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\partial B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}}\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dS\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}\frac{\log{\lvert y\rvert}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\log{\lvert y\rvert}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}

holds. Also, with
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
, and
{\lvert\nabla\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1/{\lvert x\rvert}^{3}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{(x-y)\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{2}}\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}\dfrac{\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}}\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{3}}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}\dfrac{\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}}{{\lvert x-y\rvert}}\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{3}}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\log{\lvert y\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{3}}+\frac{\log{\lvert y\rvert}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\lesssim\frac{\log{\lvert y\rvert}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}.

Summing up, with (
B.34
) we see that

(B.35)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R\log R\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.

Using this,
\partial_{1}\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y_{1}^{2},{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}>0
, we estimate as follows for
L\geq 2R
:

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\Bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y_{1}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\Bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(R\log R\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}\right){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim R^{3-2s}\log R+R^{-s}\lesssim R^{3}.

Using the above argument, we can obtain that for
\widetilde{L}>L
, we have

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\Bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s}y_{1}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\Bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\left(\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}\right){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\lesssim L^{-s}.

This proves that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\end{split}

is a Cauchy sequence for
L
. Then we see that

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{3}(y)},{{\langle{j_{2}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

exists. Similarly, using
\partial^{k}_{i}\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
k=0,1,2,3
and
i=1,2,\dots,d
, we conclude that

(B.36)

\displaystyle{\langle{(-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2})(y)},{{\langle{j_{2}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

exists.

Step 2: Estimate for
j_{2}
in the case of
d\geq 3
.
When
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq{\Bigg\lvert\int_{B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}+{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{d-2}+{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert},

where we have used
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\rvert}\lesssim 1
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Here, if
-y\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
, then employing
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\,dx\lesssim R^{-3}.

On the other hand, if
-y\not\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
so that
B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)=\emptyset
, then using
{\lvert\nabla_{x}(\varphi(x+y))\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\}
we have

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)\cup B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y))}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\,dx\lesssim R^{d-2}.

Then if
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

(B.37)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d-2}.

Now we consider the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
. Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx+\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0))}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx.

Here, using
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim 1/{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{d-s}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and recalling
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
,

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)}\frac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{d-s}}\,dx\lesssim\dfrac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{s}\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}

holds. Moreover, employing
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim 1/{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{d+1}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and using
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\geq 2
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0))}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+1}}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(-y)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0))}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\frac{1}{{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{d+1}}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{d-2}\dfrac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+1}}+\dfrac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}.

Summing up, with (
B.37
) we see that

(B.38)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d-2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2-s}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.

Using this and
\partial^{k}_{i}\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
k=0,1,2
and
i=1,2,\dots,d
, we compute for
L\geq 2R
as follows:

(B.39)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\\
&\quad\lesssim R^{d-2s}+R^{-s}\lesssim R^{d}.\end{split}

Moreover, we can obtain that for
\widetilde{L}>L
, we have

(B.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\lesssim L^{-s}.\end{split}

This proves that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\end{split}

is a Cauchy sequence for
L
. Then we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle{j_{3}(y)},{{\langle{j_{2}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}\end{split}

exists. Similarly, we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle{(-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2})(y)},{{\langle{j_{2}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}\end{split}

exists.

Step 3: Estimate for
j_{1}
.
When
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, using
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
implies
-\delta-1>-2\geq-d
, so we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq\int_{B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\lesssim R^{d-\delta-1}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx,

where for the last inequality we have used
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim 1
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Here, if
-y\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
, then using
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\,dx\lesssim R^{-\delta-2}.

On the other hand, if
-y\not\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
so that
B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)=\emptyset
, then employing
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)\cup B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y))}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim R^{-\delta-1}\cdot R^{d}+R^{-\delta-1}R^{-1}\lesssim R^{d}

holds, since
R\geq 2
. Then if
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

(B.41)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d}.

Now we consider the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
. In this case we will use the fact that the integral of the odd function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is zero instead of using divergence theorem. Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx.

Here, when
x\in B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)
, by Lemma
2.5
we observe that

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})-{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}{\lvert x_{1}\rvert}

and so together with
{\lvert x_{1}\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}\}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx+c\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}{\lvert x_{1}\varphi(x)\rvert}\,dx\\
&\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\end{split}

for some
c\geq 1
. On the other hand, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim 1/{\lvert x\rvert}^{d+1}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and recalling
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\geq 2
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx+\hskip-8.53581pt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}\hskip-14.22636pt{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\Big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}\frac{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-1}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d+1}}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}\frac{{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-1}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{d+1}}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{d-\delta-1}\cdot\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+1}}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\cdot\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\lesssim\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}.

Summing up, with (
B.41
) we see that

(B.42)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\\
&\quad\lesssim R^{d}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.\end{split}

Using this and
\partial_{1}\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we estimate as follows for
L\geq 2R
:

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R}R^{d}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,+{\bigg\lvert\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{y_{1}\partial_{1}\varphi(x)}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{\delta+2}}\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)dy\bigg\rvert}.

Here, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R}R^{d}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\lesssim R^{d-2s+2}.

Moreover, using Fubini’s theorem and observing that the integrand is odd, we find

(B.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\partial_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)dy\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\int_{\max\{2{\lvert x\rvert},R\}\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\hskip-8.53581pt\left({\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\right)\cdot\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\,dy\bigg)\partial_{1}\varphi(x)dx\\
&=0.\end{split}

Also, we see that

(B.44)

\displaystyle\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)dy\lesssim R^{-2s}.
