Using the above argument, we can obtain that for
\widetilde{L}>L
, we have

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\dfrac{y_{1}\partial_{1}\varphi(x)}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{\delta+2}}\,dx+\frac{c}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\bigg)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq L^{-2s}.

This proves that

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\,dy

is a Cauchy sequence for
L
. Then we see that

\displaystyle{\langle{j_{3}(y)},{{\langle{j_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

exists. Similarly, for each
i=1,2,\dots,d
, using
\partial^{k}_{i}\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
k=0,1,2
together with the fact that
h_{i}\mapsto{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s}h_{1}^{2}
and
h_{i}\mapsto{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}
are even, we see that

\displaystyle{\langle{(-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2})(y)},{{\langle{j_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

exists.

Considering
Step 1
–
Step 3
, we prove the conclusion.
∎

Recalling
\eta_{k}(\cdot)
as in (
B.14
), and
\xi_{j}(\cdot,\cdot)
as in (
B.15
), we prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.11
.

The convolution of distributions
g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

(B.45)

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{3})(x)},{{\langle{g_{4}(y)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}={\big\langle{g_{3}(x)},{{\langle{g_{4}(y)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}.

Proof.

Note that once the double limit in the right-hand side of (
B.45
) exists, then

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{3})(\cdot)\times g_{4}(\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.28671pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.1909pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}})},{\xi_{j}(\cdot,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.28671pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.1909pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}})\varphi(\cdot+\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.74991pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.28671pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}{\hbox to5.1909pt{\hfil\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}\hfil}}})}\rangle}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{3})(x)},{{\langle{g_{4}(y)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}

holds. Here, for the first definition since
g_{3},g_{4}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset(C^{\infty}_{c})^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\xi_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
,
[
Vla02
, Page 96 and Page 52]
is used, and for the second definition since
\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
,
[
Vla02
, Page 41]
is used. Moreover, by
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, we have
{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}={\langle{g_{4}\divideontimes g_{3}},{\varphi}\rangle}
.

Now it suffices to show that

(B.46)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{4})(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}={\big\langle{g_{4}(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}.

To do this, for
k,j>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\big\lvert{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{4})(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}-{\big\langle{g_{4}(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\big\lvert{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{4})(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{(1-\xi_{j}(x,y))\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\big\lvert{\big\langle{(1-\eta_{k})g_{4}(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

Using
\sum^{3}_{i=0}{\lvert\nabla^{i}[(1-\xi^{j}(x,y))\varphi(x+y)]\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2k
and
{\lvert y\rvert}>j
with
4k\leq j
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2k}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}>j}{\lvert\eta_{k}(x)\rvert}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}({\lvert y\rvert}^{-1-\delta}+({\lvert\log{\lvert y\rvert}\rvert}\cdot{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1})){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{2-2s}j^{-1-\delta}\underset{j\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

For
I_{2}
, we write the following integrand as
J(x,y):\mathbb{R}^{2d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
:

\displaystyle{\big\langle{(1-\eta_{k})g_{4}(x)},{{\langle{g_{3}(y)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\big\rangle}_{x}=:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J(x,y)\,dy\,dx=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}>k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J(x,y)\,dy\,dx,

where for the last equality we have used
\eta_{k}\equiv 1
when
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq k
. Then following the proof of Lemma
B.10
, we obtain (
B.35
), (
B.38
), and (
B.42
) with the choice of
y
replaced by
x
and
R=k/2
. Then following the remaining
proof of Lemma
B.10
but only considering the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
in its proof we get the analogous estimate of (
B.35
), (
B.39
), and (
B.44
) as follows:

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}>k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J(x,y)\,dy\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim k^{-2s}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Then (
B.46
) holds and we conclude the proof.
∎

Now for the standard mollifier
\overline{\varphi}(x)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
defined in (
B.18
) and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x-e_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we prove the following lemma, which rigorously establishes the connection between
f_{2}(d,s,\delta)
in (
B.2
) and
g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}
.

Lemma B.12
.

We have

f_{2}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
.

Proof.

We need to show

(B.47)

\displaystyle f_{2}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{(j_{1}-j_{2})(x)},{{\langle{(-j_{3}+\tfrac{j_{4}}{2}+j_{5}-j_{6}+\tfrac{j_{7}}{2})(y)},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)}\rangle}_{y}}\rangle}_{x}.

To do this, with
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
, let us write the integrand used for defining the distribution
g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}
tested with
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)
as
J_{\varepsilon}(x,y):\mathbb{R}^{2d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
in the following:

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy.

We divide the proof into two steps.

Step 1: Modifying the formula of
{\langle{g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
using divergence theorem.

In this step we will show that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy=\mathcal{Z}_{\varepsilon}

holds, where with

(B.48)

\displaystyle g(y)=\dfrac{-2y_{1}^{3}+|y|^{2}y_{1}^{2}-2|y|^{2}y_{1}+|y|^{4}}{2|y|^{d+2s+2}},

\mathcal{Z}_{\varepsilon}
is of the form

(B.49)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}g(y)|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\\
&\,\,+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy\\
&\,\,-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y))\,dx\,dy\\
&\,\,-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))]\,dx\,dy\end{split}

when
d=2
, and

(B.50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}g(y)(|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2})\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\\
&\quad+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}(|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2})(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy\end{split}

when
d\geq 3
.

For
\sigma\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
with
\sigma\leq\varepsilon
, and a cross-shaped region

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}:=(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cup(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\})

we will first show

(B.51)

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\iint_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy=0.

To this end, we observe that

(B.52)

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\leq\iint_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}{\lvert J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\rvert}\,dx\,dy\leq\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}}{\lvert J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\rvert}\,dx\,dy

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=(\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\cup(\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}),

where for the last inequality we have used the fact that since
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, on the set
(x,y)\in\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}
we obtain
\operatorname{supp}J(x,y)\subset\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}
.
We first consider the term originating from
{\langle{j_{1}\divideontimes j_{3}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
in the following:

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}}{\bigg\lvert{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\bigg\rvert}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\iint_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma^{d-\delta-1}+\sigma^{2-2s}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

Similarly, the term originated from
{\langle{j_{1}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
can be bounded by
\sigma^{2-2s}
. Moreover, when
d=2
, the term from
{\langle{j_{2}\divideontimes j_{3}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
is estimated as

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}}{\bigg\lvert\dfrac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\bigg\rvert}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\iint_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}\dfrac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}\dfrac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert\nabla_{x}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma^{1}{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}+\sigma^{2-2s}\lesssim\sigma^{1-s}.

The term originated from
{\langle{j_{2}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
can be similarly bounded by
\sigma^{1-s}
.
When
d\geq 3
, the term from
{\langle{j_{2}\divideontimes j_{3}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
is bounded as

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\varepsilon}}{\bigg\lvert\dfrac{1}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\bigg\rvert}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\iint_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}{\lvert\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{1-\sigma-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\sigma+\varepsilon\}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}{\lvert\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\rvert}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma^{d-2}+\sigma^{2-2s}\lesssim\sigma^{1-s}.

Note that the implicit constants in the above estimate do not depend on
\varepsilon
. Likewise, the term from
{\langle{j_{2}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
can be bounded by
\sigma^{1-s}
. Taking the limit
\sigma\to 0
proves (
B.51
) for fixed
\varepsilon
.

Now for
\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}=(\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1/\sigma\})\cup(\{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\sigma\}\times\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq 1/\sigma\})
we have to show

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\iint_{\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy=0.

To do this, note that on the set
\{x:{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}\times\{y:{\lvert y\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}\}
, we have
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=0
from
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}\}\times\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dy\,dx.

Now by following the proof of Lemma
B.10
, as in (
B.42
) with the choice of
R=\frac{2}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\leq\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}y_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx+\frac{c(d,s,\delta)}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}\end{split}

holds when
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{2}{\sigma}
. Then following the remaining proof of Lemma
B.10
, especially from (
B.43
) and (
B.44
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\iint_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}\}\times\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\sigma\}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{2s}.

The integral on the set
\{{\lvert x\rvert}>\frac{1}{\sigma}\}\times\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\sigma\}
is estimated similarly. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{\Big\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy\Big\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{2s}.

Now we are ready to apply divergence theorem. Here we divide the case
d=2
and
d\geq 3
. If
d=2
, by divergence theorem, for

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}\times\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\},

together with
j_{1}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}
,
j_{2}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}
,
j_{3}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}x_{1}^{3}
,
j_{4}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}x_{1}^{2}
,
j_{5}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}x_{1}^{2}
,
j_{6}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s}x_{1}
, and
j_{7}(x)={\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}
when
{\lvert x\rvert}>0
, (
B.32
), and using
\partial_{y_{i}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))=\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)
we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}g(y)|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad+\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))]\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}\widetilde{J}(x,y)\,dS

for some
\widetilde{J}(x,y)
. Here, we need to estimate the term
\widetilde{J}(x,y)
on the set

\displaystyle\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert x\rvert}=1/\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}=1/\sigma,\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1/\sigma\}

\displaystyle:=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

First of all, note that in the function
\widetilde{J}(x,y)
, there is no term corresponding
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{4}
and
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{7}
, since in the definition of
j_{1}
,
j_{4}
and
j_{7}
, there is no partial derivative of the test function
\varphi
in the integrand, thus in fact divergence theorem is not applied for terms
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{4}
and
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{7}
.

For the term corresponding to
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{3}
, note that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{1}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\nu(y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{I_{1}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y))\nu(y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{I_{1}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\nu(y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad=:A_{1}+A_{2}.

Here, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, and then
{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
for
{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma
and
1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma
, and
{\lvert\nu(y)\rvert}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert A_{1}\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\rvert}{\lvert\nu(y)\rvert}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{d-1-\delta}.

For
A_{2}
, since

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\,dx=0,

we have
A_{2}=0
. Then it follows that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{1}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\nu(y)\,dS\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{d-1-\delta}.

Similarly, the term for
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{5}
can be bound as

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\big\lvert{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\big\rvert}\left({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}\right)\,dS\lesssim\sigma^{d-1-\delta}.

Likewise, the term for
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{6}
will be estimated by
\sigma^{d-1-\delta}
up to a constant.

Now for the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes j_{3}
, we see the bound

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}\dfrac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}\left({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}\right)\,dS\lesssim\sigma{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}.

Similarly, for the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})
, we have the bound
\sigma{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}
. Finally,

\displaystyle\int_{I_{1}}\widetilde{J}(x,y)\,dS\lesssim\sigma^{d-1-\delta}+\sigma{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}\lesssim\sigma{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}.

For the integral on the set
I_{2}
, for the term corresponding to
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{3}
, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
we estimate

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\nu(x)\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\sigma^{\delta+1}\sigma^{d+2s-2}\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{\sigma}\right)^{d-1}\sigma^{\delta+1}\sigma^{d+2s-2}\lesssim\sigma^{\delta+2s}.

In the similar way, for the term corresponding to
j_{1}\divideontimes(j_{5}-j_{6})
can be bound by
\sigma^{\delta+2s}
. For the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes j_{3}
, using
\sigma^{s}{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}\lesssim 1
we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\frac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma}\right)^{d-1}\sigma^{d+2s-2}\leq\sigma^{s}.

Similarly, the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})
, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\frac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1})\,dS\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{s}.

For the integral on the set
I_{3}
, for the term corresponding to
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{3}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\nu(y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{I_{3}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\nu(y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{I_{3}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\nu(y)\,dS

\displaystyle\quad=:A_{3}+A_{4}.

For
A_{3}
,
using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, and then
{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}
and
{\lvert\nu(y)\rvert}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert A_{1}\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}{\lvert\nu(y)\rvert}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+3}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

On the other hand,
A_{4}=0
holds, since
\nu(y)=\frac{y_{1}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}
implies

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\nu(y)\,dy=0.

Then there holds

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{3}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\nu(y)\,dS\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

In the similar way, the term corresponding to
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{6}
can be bound by
\sigma^{2-2s}
.
Now we need to estimate the terms from
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{5}
in
\widetilde{J}(x,y)
. Together with the observation
\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)=\partial_{y_{i}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))
, the terms are of the forms

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left[{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\partial_{y_{i}}({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2})\right]\frac{y_{i}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\,dS

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}y_{i}(\partial_{i}\varphi_{\varepsilon})(x+y)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-(-d-2s+2)(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)){\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s-1}y_{i}^{2}\right]\,dx\,dy

for
i=1,2,\dots,n
. It suffices to show when
i=1
, and then the other terms estimated similarly. Note that

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}y_{1}\,dy=0

and using
{\lvert(\partial_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon})(x+y)-(\partial_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon})(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}
for
{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma
and
\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1}[y_{1}(\partial_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon})(x+y)-y_{1}(\partial_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon})(x)]\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+3}.

Also, note that

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s-1}y^{2}_{i}y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dy=0.

Then for
{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma
and
\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}
, using
{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}
from
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s-1}y_{i}^{2}{\lvert\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-y\cdot\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+3}.

Hence, together with
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
we obtain

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{3}}\hskip-2.84526pt{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left[{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\!-\!(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\!-\!\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\partial_{y_{i}}({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2})\right]\frac{y_{i}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+3}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma^{d-1}\sigma^{-d-2s+3}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

For the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes j_{3}
, using

\displaystyle{\lvert\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}={\lvert\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}

and

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\left(\frac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\dfrac{y_{1}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\nabla\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dy=0,

together with
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}}\frac{x\log{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\left(\frac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\nabla_{x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\frac{y_{1}}{{\lvert y\rvert}}\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}\frac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}{\lvert y\rvert}\,dS\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

Similarly, for the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})
, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\sigma}\int_{1-\varepsilon-\sigma\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1+\varepsilon+\sigma}\frac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+3}\,dS\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{2-2s}.

Finally, we consider the integral on
I_{4}
. For the corresponding term to
j_{1}\divideontimes j_{3}
, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{4}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\left(\dfrac{-(-d-2s+2)y_{1}^{2}+2{\lvert y\rvert}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s}(-d-2s+2)(-d-2s)}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\nu(y)\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\sigma^{\delta+1}\sigma^{d+2s-2}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\dfrac{1}{\sigma}\right)^{d-1}\sigma^{\delta+1}\sigma^{d+2s-2}\lesssim\sigma^{\delta+2s}.

Similarly, for the term corresponding to
j_{1}\divideontimes(j_{5}-j_{6})
can be bound by
\sigma^{\delta+2s}
. For the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes j_{3}
, using
\sigma^{s}{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}\lesssim 1
, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\frac{1}{\sigma}-1\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}+1}\frac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\,dS\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sigma{\lvert\log\sigma\rvert}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma}\right)^{d-1}\sigma^{d+2s-2}\leq\sigma^{s}.

Similarly, the term corresponding to
j_{2}\divideontimes(j_{4}+j_{5}-j_{6}+j_{7})
, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}=\frac{1}{\sigma}}\int_{\frac{1}{\sigma}-1\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}+1}\frac{{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}}{{\lvert x\rvert}}({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+1})\,dS\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\sigma^{s}.

When
d\geq 3
, again by divergence theorem,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}g(y)(|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2})\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\iint_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}(|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2})(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}\overline{J}(x,y)\,dS

for some
\overline{J}(x,y)
. Here, we need to estimate the term
\overline{J}(x,y)
on the set
\partial\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}
. However, its proof is similar to the case of
d=2
so we skip the proof. Overall, recalling
\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}
,
\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow 0}\left(\int_{\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy+\int_{\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy+\int_{\mathcal{F}_{\sigma}}J_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,dx\,dy\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathcal{Z}_{\varepsilon},

where with
g(y)
in (
B.48
),
\mathcal{Z}_{\varepsilon}
is of the form in (
B.49
) when
d=2
and (
B.50
) when
d\geq 3
.

Step 2: Proof of (
B.47
).
We need to show

(B.53)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{Z}_{\varepsilon}=f_{2}(d,s,\delta).

To do this, we only give the proof in the case of
d=2
since when
d\geq 3
the proof is similar. For fixed
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
, consider
\varepsilon\in(0,1/100)
with
2\varepsilon\leq\kappa
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}=\mathbb{R}^{2d}\setminus(\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}\cup\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}).

We first estimate the integration on
\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}
. Using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x-y+e_{1}|^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy.

Note that for each
x\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}+1)\,dy=0.

Then with
f(z)={\lvert x-z+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-z_{1}+1)
, using

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}+1)\rvert}={\lvert f(y)-f(0)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}

for
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon
and
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa
, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x-y+e_{1}|^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{{\lvert y\rvert}^{4}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.

Then

(B.54)

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.

Similarly, we obtain

(B.55)

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}g(y)|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.

On the other hand, using change of variables,

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}+1)-\nabla f(0)\cdot y\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\lvert f(y)-f(0)-\nabla f(0)\cdot y\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\cdot y\,dy=0,

we have

(B.56)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\\
&\,\,=\frac{c}{\varepsilon^{d}}{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x-y+e_{1}|^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\left(\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)-\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x-y}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)dxdy\bigg\rvert}\\
&\,\,=\frac{c}{\varepsilon^{d}}\bigg|\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left(|x-y+e_{1}|^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}+1)\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)dxdy\bigg|\\
&\,\,\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s+2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\varphi\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.\end{split}

Similarly, observing that
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\equiv 0
when
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\leq 1/100
, we can obtain

(B.57)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y))\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\\
&\quad+{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))]\,dy\bigg\rvert}\\
&={\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}+{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\,dy\bigg\rvert}\\
&\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.\end{split}

Note that the implicit constant in the above inequalities do not depend on
\varepsilon
.

Now we consider the integral on the set
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}
.
Using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\overline{c}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x-y+e_{1}|^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy.

To estimate further, we have

\displaystyle\overline{c}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x-y+e_{1}|^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\hskip-8.53581pt\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\hskip-8.53581pt\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\hskip-17.07164pt\int_{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\hskip-17.07164pt\left({\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,+\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert},\varepsilon\}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-1-\delta}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{d-1-\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}},

where for the first inequality we have used that

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx=0

as well as that on the set
\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon
, by Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2-\delta}{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert},

and on the set
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{2}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert},\varepsilon\}
,
{\lvert{\lvert x-y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1}+1)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert y\!-\!e_{1}\rvert}^{-1-\delta}
holds.
Hence we get

(B.58)

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{3}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\min\{\frac{1}{2},2-2s\}}.

Similarly, we obtain

(B.59)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}g(y)|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\\
&+{\bigg\lvert\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\left(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dx\,dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.\end{split}

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))]\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))\right]\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy,

where we have used the fact that when
{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa
and
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\equiv 0
, since
{\lvert x+y-e_{1}\rvert}\geq{\lvert x\rvert}-{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\geq 1-\kappa\geq\varepsilon
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{2}.

Also, when
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\equiv 0
holds. Then together with
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0)=0
since
\varepsilon\leq 1/100
, we have

(B.60)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))\right]\,dx\,dy\\
&\quad=\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\right)\,dx\,dy\\
&\quad=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\,dx\\
&\quad\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{(1-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert e_{1}-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-1\right)\,dx.\end{split}

To check the convergence as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, note that when
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq 1-2\kappa
and
{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon
, then
{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}>\kappa
since
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
and so

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{1-2\kappa\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}\leq\kappa}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa})\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx.

Here, if
y\in B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
then
{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa
so that

\displaystyle\int_{1-2\kappa\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{1-2\kappa\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\,dx\lesssim-\log(1-2\kappa)\lesssim\kappa.

Also, if
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa-\varepsilon
and
{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon
, then
{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}\leq\kappa
, thus

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}\leq\kappa}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa})\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\kappa-\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx\lesssim-\kappa^{-d-2s}\log\frac{1-2\kappa}{\kappa/2}.

Moreover, we use

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\frac{{\lvert x\rvert}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}

when
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa
and
{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\varepsilon
to obtain

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\dfrac{(y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1-2\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-1}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})}\frac{1}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\,dx\lesssim 1-2\kappa.

Note that the implicit constant in the above inequalities again do not depend on
\varepsilon
.
Thus for fixed
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
with
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
, by Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we obtain the convergence as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
in (
B.60
). Now,

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\dfrac{(1-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert e_{1}-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-1\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\dfrac{(1-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert e_{1}-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-1\right)\,dx-\int_{\kappa\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\,dx.

Here, by using
{\lvert(1-x_{1})^{2}{\lvert e_{1}-x\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}-1\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, observe that for
{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa
,

(B.61)

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\dfrac{(1-x_{1})^{2}}{{\lvert e_{1}-x\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}-1\right)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2}.

Also, we write

(B.62)

\displaystyle\int_{\kappa\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\,dx=:L_{1}(\kappa),

which is a large number since
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
is small, but it will be canceled out to the other term in the following estimate (
B.65
).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle-\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)]\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}g(y)\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y+x\rvert}\leq\kappa}g(y-x)-\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}g(y)\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}-\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}g(e_{1}-x)-g(e_{1})\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}g(e_{1}-x)-1\right)\,dx,

where the convergence
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
is justified again by Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and the similar argument as above. Now, we see that

(B.63)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}g(e_{1}-x)-1\right)\,dx\\
&\quad=-\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(g(e_{1}-x)-1\right)\,dx-L_{1}(\kappa).\end{split}

Here, by using
{\lvert g(e_{1}-x)-1\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, observe that

(B.64)

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left(g(e_{1}-x)-1\right)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2}.

Overall, merging the estimates for
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}
in (
B.58
)–(
B.64
), we have

(B.65)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\left|\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}\frac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\left[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))\right]\,dx\,dy-L_{1}(\kappa)\right.\\
&\quad\left.-\left(\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)]\,dx\,dy-L_{1}(\kappa)\right)\right|\\
&\quad\lesssim\kappa^{2-2s}.\end{split}

Now on the set
\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}
, the integrand including
g(y)
,
y_{1}^{2}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2s-2}
,
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2-\delta}x_{1}
, and
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}
are no longer singular, so we can apply the argument of the proof of
[
Eva10
, Page 714, Theorem 7]
. In detail, recalling
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
and
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, from

\displaystyle(x,y)\in\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}\,\,\implies\,\,{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\,\,\implies\,\,\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)\equiv 0,

together with
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0)\equiv 0
(also from
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
) we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}\left(\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(0))]\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-2}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\left({\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))-{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-2}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\left({\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}-{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-2}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy.

Then by the argument in
[
Eva10
, Page 715]
, we see that the first term in the rightmost term in the above converges to zero as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
by Lebesgue differentiation theorem. Moreover, the second term is computed as

(B.66)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:=\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\,dx\,dy\,\,\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\,\,\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\,dy=:L_{2}(\kappa)

which is a large number since
\kappa
is small, but it will be canceled out to the other term which appear soon. Similarly,

(B.67)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}:=&\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}\left(-\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x))\right.\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s+2}}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-2-\delta}(e_{1}-y)\right)\,dx\,dy\\
&\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}-\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y-e_{1}\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\dfrac{y_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+2s+2}}\,dy=-L_{2}(\kappa).\end{split}

Thus both
L_{2}(\kappa)
is canceled out and so we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathcal{P}+\mathcal{Q}=0.

The other terms estimated similarly so that the following term

\displaystyle\bigg|\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}g(y)|x|^{-2-\delta}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}g(y){\lvert x\rvert}^{-2}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)-\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1\}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y))\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad-\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}g(y)\left(|e_{1}-y|^{-2-\delta}(1-y_{1})-{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-2}\right)dy\bigg|

converges to 0 as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Note that

\displaystyle f_{2}(d,s,\delta)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\kappa\rightarrow 0}\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}g(y)\left(|e_{1}-y|^{-2-\delta}(1-y_{1})-{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}^{-2}\right)dy,

where
g(y)
is defined in (
B.48
).

Therefore, recalling the proof of Lemma
3.1
so that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\,\mathrm{p.v.}\!\!\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\\
\cup{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\end{subarray}}\dfrac{-2y_{1}^{3}+|y|^{2}y_{1}^{2}+2y_{1}^{2}-2|y|^{2}y_{1}+|y|^{4}}{2|y|^{d+2s+2}|e_{1}-y|^{2}}\left(|e_{1}-y|^{-\delta}(1-y_{1})-1\right)dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim o(\kappa),

the estimates of the integral on
\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}
bounded by
\kappa^{2-2s}
respectively by (
B.54
)–(
B.57
), (
B.65
)–(
B.67
), and also noticing that each
L_{2}(\kappa)
appearing in (
B.66
) and (
B.67
) cancels out each other, we have obtained the desired estimate (
B.53
) since
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
is arbitrary chosen and
\varepsilon
is already sent to zero. The proof is complete.
∎

Now a computation gives
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}](\xi)=\mathcal{G}_{3}(\xi)
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{4}](\xi)=c\mathcal{G}_{4}(\xi)
for some
c=c(d,s)
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}(\xi):=\begin{cases}-i\pi^{\delta+1-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1}-2\pi\log|\xi|+2\pi(\log 2-\gamma)\,\,&\text{when }d=2\\
-i\pi^{\delta+1-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1}+\pi^{2-\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)|\xi|^{-d+2}\,\,&\text{when }d\neq 2\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\dfrac{-4i(1-s)}{\pi(d+2s)}|x|^{2s-4}x_{1}^{3}+\dfrac{s-1}{\pi^{2}}|x|^{2s-4}x_{1}^{2}-\dfrac{4}{d+2s}|x|^{2s-2}x_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{i(2d+4s+6)}{\pi(d+2s)}|x|^{2s-2}x_{1}+\dfrac{d+2s-1}{2\pi^{2}}|x|^{2s-2}-\dfrac{2}{s(d+2s)}|x|^{2s}

are locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma B.13
.

We have

(B.68)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{3}](\xi)=\mathcal{G}_{3}(\xi)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}[g_{4}](\xi)=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s+1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\mathcal{G}_{4}(\xi)

in the distributional sense.

Proof.

The first term of the right-hand side of the above can be calculated as follows: when
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-\delta-2}h_{1}\right](\xi)

\displaystyle=\left(\dfrac{i}{2\pi}\right)\partial_{1}\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-\delta-2}\right](\xi)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{i}{2\pi}\pi^{\delta+2-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta-2}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}\partial_{1}(|\xi|^{-d+\delta+2})

\displaystyle=-i\pi^{\delta+1-\frac{d}{2}}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d-\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\delta+2}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1}.

When
\delta=0
, we still obtain the above equality using the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[{\lvert h\rvert}^{-2}h_{1}](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[\partial_{1}(\log{\lvert h\rvert})]=2\pi i\xi_{1}\mathcal{F}[\log{\lvert h\rvert}]

\displaystyle=2\pi i\xi_{1}\left(-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}{2\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}\frac{1}{{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{d}}+\left(\log 2+\frac{\psi\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)-\gamma}{2}\right)\delta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=-i\pi^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right){\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d}\xi_{1},

where
\psi(\cdot)
is the digamma function,
\gamma
is the Euler-Mascheroni constant, and
\delta_{0}
is the Dirac delta distribution concentrated at 0, and for the third equality we have used
[
CW19
, Remark 1.2]
, and for the last line we have used
\xi_{1}\delta\equiv 0
.

We also see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-2}\right]=\begin{cases}\pi^{2-\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)|\xi|^{-d+2}&\quad d\neq 2\\
-2\pi\log|\xi|+2\pi(\log 2-\gamma)&\quad d=2.\end{cases}

Here, in case of
d=2
, from
[
CW19
, Remark 1.2]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\frac{1}{|\xi|^{d}}\right](x)=\dfrac{2\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}\left(-\log|x|+\log 2+\frac{\psi\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)-\gamma}{2}\right)

in the distributional sense, where
\psi(\cdot)
is the digamma function and
\gamma=-\Gamma^{\prime}(1)
is the Euler-Mascheroni constant.

Subsequent computations yield

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{-2h_{1}^{3}}{|h|^{d+2s+2}}\right]

\displaystyle=-2\left(\dfrac{i}{2\pi}\right)^{3}\partial_{1}^{3}\mathcal{F}\left[|h|^{-d-2s-2}\right]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2i}{(2\pi)^{3}}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s+2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s+2}{2}\right)}\partial_{1}^{3}(|\xi|^{2s+2})

\displaystyle=i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{2}{d+2s}\left(2(s-1)|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{3}+3|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}\right).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{h_{1}^{2}}{|h|^{d+2s}}\right]=\dfrac{1}{2}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-2}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\left(2(s-1)|\xi|^{2s-4}\xi_{1}^{2}+|\xi|^{2s-2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{2h_{1}^{2}}{|h|^{d+2s+2}}\right]=-\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\frac{2}{d+2s}\left(2|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{s}|\xi|^{2s}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{-2h_{1}}{|h|^{d+2s}}\right]=2i\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(-s+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}|\xi|^{2s-2}\xi_{1},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\dfrac{1}{|h|^{d+2s-2}}\right]=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+2s-2}\dfrac{\Gamma(-s+1)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2s}{2}\right)}\dfrac{d+2s-2}{2}|\xi|^{2s-2}.

Merging the terms calculated above, we obtain (
A.1
).
∎

Now with
\phi_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)
with
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
where
\overline{\varphi}
is a standard mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma B.14
.

The multiplication of distributions
\mathcal{G}_{3}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{4}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{3}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{4}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}.

Moreover, we have

(B.69)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{4}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{G}_{3}\mathcal{G}_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle},

where
\mathcal{G}_{3}\mathcal{G}_{4}
means the usual multiplication.

Proof.

Let us choose
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
. We first prove for the case of
d=2
. Consider

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\hskip-5.69054pt\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\hskip-5.69054pt\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[({\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}y_{1}+\log{\lvert y\rvert})\!-\!({\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}x_{1}+\log{\lvert x\rvert})\right]\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}({\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}x_{1}+\log{\lvert x\rvert})\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\bigg\lvert\overline{c}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)({\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})+\log{\lvert x-y\rvert})\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad=:{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}+{\lvert\overline{c}I_{2}\rvert}.

For
I_{1}
, we estimate

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}x_{1}\left({\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{3}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}x_{1}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}x_{1}^{2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2s+\delta},

and

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\log{\lvert x\rvert}\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{s}.

Also, we see that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}(x_{1}^{2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}x\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}(x_{1}^{2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}x\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\lesssim\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta+2s}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}.

Here, for the last estimate we have used the fact that the map

\displaystyle x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}(x_{1}^{2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}\frac{\varphi(x)+\varphi(-x)}{2}x

is odd, and that since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
holds. Then we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}(x_{1}^{2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}.

Summing up the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{s}.

Also for
I_{2}
, since
2s-2<0
we estimate

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}{\lvert\log{\lvert x-y\rvert}\rvert}\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}{\lvert\log{\lvert x\rvert}\rvert}\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{s}.

For the terms
{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{3}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}x_{1}^{2}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}x_{1}+{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s}
in
\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)
, we see that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-1}{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta+1}\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{2s+\delta}

holds, and for the terms
{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{2}
and
{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}
in
\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)
, we observe that

\displaystyle x\mapsto\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x-y)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy

is odd, and so the map

\displaystyle x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{2}\frac{\varphi(x)+\varphi(-x)}{2}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x-y)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right)

is odd. Moreover, since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
holds. Using this, we see that

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{2}\varphi(x)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x-y)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{2}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x-y)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\bigg\lvert\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-1}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta+1}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\right)\,dy\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-4}x_{1}^{2}\varphi(x)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2s-2}\varphi(x)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d+\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta+2s}

holds. Then merging the estimates, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{3}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{s}.

Note that the implicit constants in the above estimates do not depend on
\varepsilon
.
