Now similar to the proof of Lemma
B.12
, since on the set
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa
with
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
, both
\mathcal{G}_{3}(x)
and
\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)
ar no longer singular, by Lebesgue differentiation theorem we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{3}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}=0.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\lvert{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{4}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}-{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{3}\mathcal{G}_{4}},{\varphi}\rangle}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{3}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{3}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{3}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{4}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{s}.

Since
\kappa
was arbitrary, we obtain (
B.69
) in case of
d=2
. In case of
d=3
is similar so we skip the proof.
∎

With the help of Lemma
A.2
and
B.14
, we have the following theorem.

Theorem B.15
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]

in the distributional sense, where
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
.

Proof.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, note that

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow\infty 0}{\langle{(\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow\infty 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}](y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{F}[g_{4}](x)},{\varphi(x)}\big\rangle}_{x}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]},{\varphi}\rangle},

where for the first equality since
g_{3},g_{4}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have used
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
together with Lemma
B.10
and Lemma
B.11
, for the second equality since
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
as in
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
(
\theta_{M}
is defined in (
B.31
)) and
\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
(\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
holds as in
[
Vla02
, Page 79]
, for the third equality again since
\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
,
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
is used, and for the last equality Lemma
B.14
together with Lemma
A.2
is used.
∎

Then it is worth mentioning the following corollary.

Corollary B.16
.

We have
(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}]=\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]\right]
.

Proof.

Since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by Theorem
B.15
we have

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}\divideontimes g_{4}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{3}]\mathcal{F}[g_{4}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle},

which proves the conclusion.
∎

B.3.
Convolution theorem for
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)

We define the distribution
g_{7}
, whose convolution with
g_{6}
will recover
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)
. Note that from now on, we assume

\displaystyle\delta\in[0,\tfrac{d}{2}).

Definition B.17
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define the distribution
g_{5}
and
g_{6}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{5}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{x_{1}}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx,

and

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1+s}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\varphi(x)\,dx.

Note that the above distributions are well-defined with
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
g_{5}
and
g_{6}
can be understood as locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The main goal of this subsection is to show the following relation (see Theorem
B.25
):

(B.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]-ic_{1}(d,s)(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}

in the distributional sense, where
\divideontimes
is a convolution of two distributions in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
introduced in
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, and
\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
and
c_{1}(d,s)
is defined in Lemma
B.24
. The well-definedness of the left-hand side term of (
B.70
) will be proved in Lemma
B.18
and Lemma
B.19
, and the well-definedness of the right-hand side term of (
B.70
) will be proved in Lemma
B.24
. We emphasize that the additional term involving
(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})
in (
B.70
) is only necessary for
\delta=0
case, see Lemma
B.24
and its proof below. This additional term with
(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})
is different from other results such as (
B.33
) and (
B.80
), however this difference does not change much the application in Lemma
B.14
.

To show (
B.70
), first we prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.18
.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\langle{g_{5}(y)},{{\langle{g_{6}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}
is well-defined, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{5}(y)},{{\langle{g_{6}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

exists for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.

Let us choose
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Note that for each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Since
y\mapsto{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}
is even and
x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}
is odd, observe that

(B.71)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\\
&\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\right)\,dy.\end{split}

Let
R\geq 2
. When
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d+s-\delta}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx.

Here, if
-y\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
, then using
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-1}\,dx\lesssim R^{-1+s}.

On the other hand, if
-y\not\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
so that
B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)=\emptyset
, then employing
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-s}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)\cup B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y))}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-s}\,dx\lesssim R^{d+s}

holds, since
R\geq 2
. The integral with the integrand
{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi(-x-y)
in (
B.71
) is estimated similarly. Hence if
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

(B.72)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d+s}.

Now we consider the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
. Using change of variables, let us write

(B.73)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\,dy\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\hskip-2.84526pt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}\hskip-2.84526pt\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)dx.\end{split}

Here, using

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\lvert{\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}y_{1}-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})+{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}y_{1}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2},

for
x\in B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}\varphi(x)\,dx\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}.

For another term, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}\eqsim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2}
for
x\in B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{d+s-\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-2}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}.

For the other term, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}\left({\lvert y+x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+x_{1})-{\lvert y-x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}-x_{1})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}.

Summing up, with (
B.72
) we see that

(B.74)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\lesssim\,R^{d}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.\end{split}

Then together with
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}>0
, we estimate as follows for
L\geq 2R
:

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R}R^{d}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\,dy+\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{d+1-s}+R^{-\delta-1}\lesssim R^{d+1}.

Also, for
\widetilde{L}>L
, we have

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\,dy\lesssim L^{-\delta-1}.

This proves that

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\right)\,dy

is a Cauchy sequence for
L
. Then we see that

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{2}(y)},{{\langle{g_{1}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\dfrac{\varphi(x+y)-\varphi(-x-y)}{2}\,dx\right)\,dy

exists, so we prove the conclusion.
∎

Recalling
\eta_{k}(\cdot)
in (
B.14
), and
\xi_{j}(\cdot,\cdot)
in (
B.15
), we have the following lemma. Its proof is similar to Lemma
B.3
and
B.11
so we skip the proof.

Lemma B.19
.

The convolution of distributions
g_{1}\divideontimes g_{2}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

(B.75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle{g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}&={\langle{g_{6}\divideontimes g_{5}},{\varphi}\rangle}\\
&:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{6})(y)},{{\langle{g_{5}(x)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.\end{split}

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}={\big\langle{g_{6}(y)},{{\langle{g_{5}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.

Here, we show that
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)
exists.

Lemma B.20
.

The function
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)
is well-defined in the sense that

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh=c(d,s,\delta)\in[0,\infty).

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle U_{R}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh.

Now, let
R>r\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle U_{R}-U_{r}

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}<\frac{1}{r}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\coloneqq\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}.

We can see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}<\frac{1}{r}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\lesssim r^{s-1}.

Also, for
\mathrm{II}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1-h_{1})+{\lvert e_{1}+h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(1+h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert h\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\lesssim r^{-\delta-1},

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle{\lvert{\lvert h+e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(h_{1}+1)-{\lvert h-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(h_{1}-1)\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\lvert{\lvert h+e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(h_{1}+1)-{\lvert h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}h_{1}-{\lvert h-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(h_{1}-1)+{\lvert h\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}h_{1}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\lvert h\rvert}^{s-\delta-2}

for
{\lvert h\rvert}\geq r\geq 2
. This proves that
U_{R}
is a Cauchy sequence for
R\to\infty
. Thus
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{R\to\infty}U_{R}
exists. This proves the lemma.
∎

Corollary B.21
.

For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
the limit

\displaystyle F_{1}(x):=\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert x-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{x_{1}-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh

exists. Moreover,
F_{1}(\cdot)\in L^{1}(B_{1}(0))
and

\displaystyle{\lvert F_{1}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}.

Proof.

For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, by change of variables, we can write

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert x-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{x_{1}-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh={\lvert x\rvert}^{1-\delta}\int_{\frac{{\lvert x\rvert}}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq{\lvert x\rvert}R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{s-\delta}\widehat{1-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh.

Applying a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
B.20
yields the conclusions. When
x=0
, then
F_{1}(0)=0
since the map
h\mapsto{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-\delta}h_{1}
is odd. This completes the proof.
∎

Now we prove the following lemma, which rigorously establishes the connection between
f_{3}(d,s,\delta)
and
g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}
.

Lemma B.22
.

We have

f_{3}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
.

Proof.

To do this, with
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
, we observe that

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy.

For fixed
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
, consider
\varepsilon\in(0,1/100)
with
2\varepsilon\leq\kappa
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}=\mathbb{R}^{2d}\setminus(\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}\cup\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}).

We first estimate the integration on
\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}
. Using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\frac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{1-s}.

On the set
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}
, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{1}(0)
and
\{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\}\subset\{{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq 2\kappa\}
for each
x\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, we compute

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta}\,dy\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta}\,dy\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{d+s-\delta}.

On the set
\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}
, the integrand
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)
are no longer singular, so we can apply the argument of the proof of
[
Eva10
, Page 714, Theorem 7]
. In detail, we compute

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\,\,-\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert y+e_{1}\rvert}^{s-\delta-1}(y_{1}+1)\,dy.

Since
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
is arbitrary chosen and
\varepsilon
is already sent to zero, summing up above three estimates yields the conclusion.
∎

Now we have the following.

Lemma B.23
.

We have

(B.76)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}[g_{5}](\xi)=-\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}-s+\delta}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}\xi_{1}:=\mathcal{G}_{5}(\xi)\\
&\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}[g_{6}](\xi)=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma(\frac{-s+1}{2})}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{s-1}:=\mathcal{G}_{6}(\xi)\end{split}

in the distributional sense.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{5}](\xi)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{s-\delta+1}\mathcal{F}[\partial_{1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta+1}]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2\pi i\xi_{1}}{s-\delta+1}\mathcal{F}[{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-\delta+1}]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2\pi i\xi_{1}}{s-\delta+1}\dfrac{(2\pi)^{-s+\delta-1}\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}2^{-s+\delta-1}\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta-1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}

\displaystyle=-\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}-s+\delta}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta+1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}\xi_{1}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{6}](\xi)=\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+s-1}\dfrac{\Gamma(\frac{-s+1}{2})}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{s-1}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Now with
\phi_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)
with
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
where
\overline{\varphi}
is a standard mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma B.24
.

The multiplication of distributions
\mathcal{G}_{5}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{6}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{5}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{5}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{6}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}.

Moreover, we have

(B.77)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{5}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{6}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{G}_{5}\mathcal{G}_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{5}\mathcal{G}_{6}=-\pi^{\delta-1}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}\xi_{1}-ic_{1}(d,s)(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}

with
\partial_{1}\delta_{0}
being the partial derivative with respect to
x_{1}
of Dirac delta distribution concentrated at 0, and

\displaystyle c_{1}(d,s)=\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+1-2s}(d+s-1)}{d\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}.

Proof.

Let us choose
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
and consider

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}x_{1}\right]\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\bigg\lvert\overline{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\varphi(x){\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1})\,dx\right)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad=:{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}+{\lvert\overline{c}I_{2}\rvert}.

Using the fact that
x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}
is odd, and
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, note that

(B.78)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}&={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx\bigg\rvert}\\
&=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{\delta}.\end{split}

For
I_{2}
, using the fact that the map

\displaystyle x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\dfrac{\varphi(x)+\varphi(-x)}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1})\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy

is odd since
\phi_{\varepsilon}
is even, we write

(B.79)

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1})\,dx\right)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy.

Now assume that
\delta>0
. The case of
\delta=0
will be considered later. We write

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx.

Let us estimate the first term in the right-hand side. We have

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx,

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\,dy=0.

Using
{\lvert\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1-d}{\lvert y\cdot\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rvert}
, we further compute

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta+1}\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s}\,dx\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta},

where we have used
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
. For the other term, using
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s}\varepsilon^{\delta-s}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\right)\,dx\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta}.

Note that the implicit constants in the above estimates do not depend on
\varepsilon
. Also, using
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
and
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{{\lvert x-y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\overline{c}\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}(x_{1}-y_{1})\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\overline{c}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+\delta}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{\delta}.

Then merging the estimates, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{1}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta}.

Now similar to the proof of Lemma
B.12
, since on the set
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa
with
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
, both
\mathcal{G}_{5}(x)
and
\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)
are no longer singular, by Lebesgue differentiation theorem we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{5}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{5}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}=0.

Then together with (
B.78
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\lvert{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{5}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{6}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}-{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{5}\mathcal{G}_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{5}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{5}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{5}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{5}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{\delta}.

Since
\kappa
was arbitrary, we get (
B.77
) in case of
\delta>0
.

In case of
\delta=0
, using change of variables, from (
B.77
) we estimate

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\dfrac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-s-1}(x_{1}-y_{1})\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{\frac{\varepsilon}{\kappa}}(0)}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}\kappa^{d}\kappa^{s-1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\varphi(\kappa x)\kappa^{-d-s}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-s-1}(x_{1}-y_{1})\,dx\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{\kappa y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\dfrac{1}{\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\varphi(\kappa x){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-s-1}x_{1}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\dfrac{1}{\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}\varphi(\kappa x){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}x_{1}\,dx.

Estimating further, using the fact that
x\mapsto\frac{\varphi(\kappa x)+\varphi(-\kappa x)}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}x_{1}
is odd, we see that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}\varphi(\kappa x){\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}x_{1}\,dx

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-2}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(\kappa x)-\varphi(-\kappa x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{\kappa}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(\kappa x)-\varphi(-\kappa x)}{2\kappa{\lvert x\rvert}}\,dx

\displaystyle\underset{\kappa\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}x_{1}x\cdot\nabla\varphi(0)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\partial_{1}\varphi(0)\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}x_{1}^{2}\,dx.

Here, using
[
Fol01
]
, we calculate

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}x_{1}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+1}\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{{\lvert x\rvert}^{2}}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{S^{d}}\kappa_{1}^{2}\,d\kappa\right)r^{-d+1}r^{d-1}\,dr=\int_{S^{d}}\kappa_{1}^{2}\,d\kappa=\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2}{2}\right)}.

Thus we have obtained

\displaystyle\lim_{\kappa\rightarrow 0}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}I_{2}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2}{2}\right)}\partial_{1}\varphi(0)=\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2}{2}\right)}{\langle{\partial_{1}\delta_{0}},{\varphi}\rangle}

so that recalling the coefficients of
{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d-s+\delta-1}\xi_{1}
and
{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{s-1}
in (
B.76
), finally we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{5}\mathcal{G}_{6}=\pi^{1-2s+\delta}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)}\left({\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}\xi_{1}+\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+2}{2}\right)}(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi^{1-2s+\delta}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{-s+\delta+1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta-2}\xi_{1}\!+\!\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}+1-2s}(d+s-1)i}{d\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}.

The proof is complete.
∎

With the help of Lemma
B.23
and
B.24
, we have the following theorem.

Theorem B.25
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]-ic_{1}(d,s)(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}

in the distributional sense, where
\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
,
\partial_{1}\delta_{0}
means the partial derivative with respect to
x_{1}
of Dirac delta distribution concentrated at 0, and
c_{1}(d,s)
is defined in Lemma
B.24
.

Proof.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, note that

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{(\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}](y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{F}[g_{6}](x)},{\varphi(x)}\big\rangle}_{x}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]+ic_{1}(d,s)(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}},{\varphi}\rangle},

where for the first equality since
g_{5},g_{6}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have used
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
together with Lemma
B.18
and Lemma
B.19
, for the second equality since
\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
as in
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
(
\theta_{M}
is defined in (
B.31
)) and
\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
(\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
holds as in
[
Vla02
, Page 79]
, for the third equality again since
\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
,
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
is used, and for the last equality Lemma
B.24
together with Lemma
B.23
is used.
∎

Then it is worth mentioning the following corollary.

Corollary B.26
.

We have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}]=\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]-ic_{1}(d,s)(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}\right].

Proof.

Since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by Theorem
B.25
we have

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{5}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]+ic_{1}(d,s)(\partial_{1}\delta_{0})\mathrm{Id}_{\{\delta=0\}}},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle},

which proves the conclusion.
∎

B.4.
Convolution theorem for
f_{4}(d,s,\delta)

We define the distribution
g_{7}
, where its convolution with
g_{6}
will recover
f_{4}(d,s,\delta)
. Recall that here we also assume
\delta\in[0,\tfrac{d}{2})
.

Definition B.27
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define the distribution
g_{7}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{7}},{\varphi}\rangle}:={\langle{{\lvert x\rvert}^{-1-\delta}\widehat{x_{1}}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx.

Note that the above distribution is well-defined with
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
g_{7}
can be understood as locally integrable functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The main goal of this subsection is to show the following relation (see Theorem
B.35
):

(B.80)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]

in the distributional sense, where
\divideontimes
is a convolution of two distributions in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
introduced in
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
, and
\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
. The well-definedness of the left-hand side term of (
B.80
) will be proved in Lemma
B.28
and Lemma
B.29
, and the well-definedness of the right-hand side term of (
B.80
) will be proved in Lemma
B.34
. Overall, proofs are similar to those of the previous subsection, but for the completeness we give proofs.

To show (
B.80
), first we prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.28
.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\langle{g_{6}(y)},{{\langle{g_{7}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}
is well-defined, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{6}(y)},{{\langle{g_{7}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

exists for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.

Let us choose
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
R\geq 2
. Note that for each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we see
{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. When
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d-\delta-1}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx.

Here, if
-y\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
, then using
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-s}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-s}\,dx\lesssim R^{-1-s}.

On the other hand, if
-y\not\in B_{\frac{3}{4}R}(0)
so that
B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)\cap B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)=\emptyset
, then employing
{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\lesssim\min\{1,{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-s}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert\varphi(x+y)\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y)}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{1}{2}R}(0)\cup B_{\frac{1}{4}R}(-y))}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert x+y\rvert}^{-d-s}\,dx\lesssim R^{d-1}

holds, since
R\geq 2
. Hence if
{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R
, we obtain

(B.81)

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\Bigg\rvert}\lesssim R^{d+s}.

Now we consider the case of
{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R
. Note that

(B.82)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dy\\
&=\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad+\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)dx.\end{split}

Here, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)}\varphi(x)\,dx\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}.

For another term, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d}\eqsim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d}
for
x\in B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y)}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{d-\delta-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}.

For the other term, using
{\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-1}
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus(B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(0)\cup B_{\frac{{\lvert y\rvert}}{2}}(y))}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1})\varphi(x)\,dx\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-\delta-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}\lesssim{\lvert y\rvert}^{-2}.

Summing up, with (
B.81
) we see that

(B.83)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\lesssim\,R^{d+s}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R\}}+{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq R\}}.\end{split}

Then together with
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}>0
, we estimate as follows for
L\geq 2R
:

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq R}R^{d}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\,dy+\int_{R\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{d+1-s}+R^{-s}\lesssim R^{d+1}.

Also, for
\widetilde{L}>L
, we have

\displaystyle{\Bigg\lvert\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy\Bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{L\leq{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\widetilde{L}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-1}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\,dy\lesssim L^{-s}.

This proves that

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq L}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

is a Cauchy sequence for
L
. Then we see that

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{6}(y)},{{\langle{g_{7}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\rangle}_{y}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy

exists, so we prove the conclusion.
∎

Recalling
\eta_{k}(\cdot)
in (
B.14
), and
\xi_{j}(\cdot,\cdot)
in (
B.15
), we have the following lemma. Its proof is similar to Lemma
B.3
and
B.11
so we skip the proof.

Lemma B.29
.

The convolution of distributions
g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

(B.84)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\langle{g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}&={\langle{g_{6}\divideontimes g_{7}},{\varphi}\rangle}\\
&:=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\big\langle{(\eta_{k}g_{6})(y)},{{\langle{g_{7}(x)},{\xi_{j}(x,y)\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.\end{split}

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}={\big\langle{g_{6}(y)},{{\langle{g_{7}(x)},{\varphi(x+y)}\rangle}_{x}}\big\rangle}_{y}.

Lemma B.30
.

The function
f_{4}(d,s,\delta)
is well-defined in the sense that

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh=c(d,s,\delta)\in[0,\infty).

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle U_{R}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh.

Now, let
R>r\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle U_{R}-U_{r}

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}<\frac{1}{r}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\coloneqq\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}.

If
\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}<\frac{1}{r}
, then
{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}\eqsim 1
, so we can see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}<\frac{1}{r}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-1}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\lesssim r^{s-1}.

Also, for
\mathrm{II}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(1-h_{1})}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\lesssim\int_{r<{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert h\rvert}^{-\delta-1}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh\lesssim r^{-\delta-s}

for
{\lvert h\rvert}\geq r\geq 2
. This proves that
U_{R}
is a Cauchy sequence for
R\to\infty
. Thus
f_{4}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{R\to\infty}U_{R}
exists. This proves the lemma.
∎

Corollary B.31
.

For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
the limit

\displaystyle F_{1}(x):=\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert x-h\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\widehat{x_{1}-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh

exists. Moreover,
F_{1}(\cdot)\in L^{1}(B_{1}(0))
and

\displaystyle{\lvert F_{1}(x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}^{-s-\delta}.

Proof.

For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, by change of variables, we can write

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R}\dfrac{{\lvert x-h\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\widehat{x_{1}-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh={\lvert x\rvert}^{-s-\delta}\int_{\frac{{\lvert x\rvert}}{R}\leq{\lvert h\rvert}\leq R{\lvert x\rvert}}\dfrac{{\lvert e_{1}-h\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\widehat{1-h_{1}}}{{\lvert h\rvert}^{d-1+s}}\,dh.

Applying a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
B.30
yields the conclusions. When
x=0
, then
F_{1}(0)=0
since the map
h\mapsto{\lvert h\rvert}^{-d-s-\delta}h_{1}
is odd. This completes the proof.
∎

Now we prove the following lemma, which rigorously establishes the connection between
f_{4}(d,s,\delta)
and
g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}
.

Lemma B.32
.

We have

f_{4}(d,s,\delta)=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}
.

Proof.

To do this, with
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10})
, we observe that

\displaystyle{\langle{g_{5}\divideontimes g_{6}},{\varphi_{\varepsilon}}\rangle}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\right)\,dy.

For fixed
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
, consider
\varepsilon\in(0,1/100)
with
2\varepsilon\leq\kappa
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}:{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}=\mathbb{R}^{2d}\setminus(\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}\cup\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}).

We first estimate the integration on
\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}
. Using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{\varepsilon}(e_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{G}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,=\frac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\,\,\lesssim\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy\lesssim\kappa^{1-s}.

On the set
\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}
, using
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset B_{1}(0)
and
\{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa\}\subset\{{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq 2\kappa\}
for each
x\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, we compute

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{H}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=\dfrac{\overline{c}}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\,dy\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-1}\,dy\right)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{d-\delta-1}.

On the set
\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}
, the integrand
{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)
are no longer singular, so we can apply the argument of the proof of
[
Eva10
, Page 714, Theorem 7]
. In detail, we compute

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{I}_{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta-2}x_{1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert x-y-e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(x_{1}-y_{1}-1)\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\,\,-\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\lvert e_{1}-y\rvert}\geq\kappa\\
\cap{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\kappa\end{subarray}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d+1-s}{\lvert y+e_{1}\rvert}^{-\delta-2}(y_{1}+1)\,dy.

Since
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
is arbitrary chosen and
\varepsilon
is already sent to zero, summing up above three estimates yields the conclusion.
∎

Now we have the following.

Lemma B.33
.

We have

(B.85)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}[g_{7}](\xi)=-\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}-\delta+1}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{2-\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta}\xi_{1}:=\mathcal{G}_{7}(\xi)\end{split}

in the distributional sense.

Proof.

For
\delta>0
, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{5}](\xi)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{-\delta}\mathcal{F}[\partial_{1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}]

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{2\pi i\xi_{1}}{\delta}\mathcal{F}[{\lvert x\rvert}^{-\delta}]

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{2\pi i\xi_{1}}{\delta}\dfrac{(2\pi)^{-\delta}\Gamma\left(\frac{d+\delta}{2}\right)}{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}2^{-\delta}\Gamma\left(\frac{-\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+\delta}

\displaystyle=-\pi^{-\frac{d}{2}-\delta+1}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+\delta}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{2-\delta}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d-\delta}\xi_{1}.

When
\delta=0
, we still obtain the above equality using the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[{\lvert h\rvert}^{-2}h_{1}](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[\partial_{1}(\log{\lvert h\rvert})]=2\pi i\xi_{1}\mathcal{F}[\log{\lvert h\rvert}]

\displaystyle=2\pi i\xi_{1}\left(-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}{2\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}\frac{1}{{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{d}}+\left(\log 2+\frac{\psi\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)-\gamma}{2}\right)\delta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=-i\pi^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right){\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d}\xi_{1},

where
\psi(\cdot)
is the digamma function,
\gamma
is the Euler-Mascheroni constant, and
\delta_{0}
is the Dirac delta distribution concentrated at 0, and for the third equality we have used
[
CW19
, Remark 1.2]
, and for the last line we have used
\xi_{1}\delta\equiv 0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Now with
\phi_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)
with
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
where
\overline{\varphi}
is a standard mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma B.34
.

The multiplication of distributions
\mathcal{G}_{7}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{6}
is well-defined in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{7}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{7}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{6}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}.

Moreover, we have

(B.86)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{7}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{6}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{G}_{7}\mathcal{G}_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{7}\mathcal{G}_{6}=\pi^{s-\delta}i\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{d+\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1-s}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{2-\delta}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{d+s-1}{2}\right)}{\lvert\xi\rvert}^{-d+s+\delta-1}\xi_{1}

means the usual multiplication.

Proof.

Let us choose
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
and consider

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}-{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-\delta}x_{1}\right]\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-\delta+s-1}x_{1}\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\bigg\lvert\overline{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\varphi(x){\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\,dx\right)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad=:{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}+{\lvert\overline{c}I_{2}\rvert}.

Using the fact that
x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-\delta+s-1}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}
is odd, and
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, note that

(B.87)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\lvert I_{1}\rvert}&={\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-\delta+s-1}x_{1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx\bigg\rvert}\\
&=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-\delta+s+1}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{1+s-\delta}.\end{split}

For
I_{2}
, using the fact that the map

\displaystyle x\mapsto{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\dfrac{\varphi(x)+\varphi(-x)}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy

is odd since
\phi_{\varepsilon}
is even, we write

(B.88)

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\,dx\right)\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy.

We write

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx.

Let us estimate the first term in the right-hand side. We have

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx,

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\,dy=0.

Using
{\lvert\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1-d}{\lvert y\cdot\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rvert}
, we further compute

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\phi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta+2}\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s}\,dx\lesssim\varepsilon^{s+1-\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{s+1-\delta},

where we have used
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
. For the other term, using
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\lvert x\rvert}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq 2\varepsilon}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\left(\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}\phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\right)\,dx\lesssim\varepsilon^{s+1-\delta}\lesssim\kappa^{s+1-\delta}.

Note that the implicit constants in the above estimates do not depend on
\varepsilon
. Also, using
\operatorname{supp}\phi_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
and
{\lvert\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)\rvert}\lesssim{\lvert x\rvert}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{{\lvert x-y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}y_{1}\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\overline{c}\int_{2\varepsilon\leq{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{{\lvert y\rvert}\leq\varepsilon}{\lvert x-y\rvert}^{-d-\delta}(x_{1}-y_{1})\overline{\varphi}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy\right){\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}\overline{c}\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d-\delta+1}{\lvert x\rvert}^{s-1}\frac{\varphi(x)-\varphi(-x)}{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}{\lvert x\rvert}^{-d+s-\delta+1}\,dx\lesssim\kappa^{s+1-\delta}.

Then merging the estimates, we have

\displaystyle{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{1}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{2}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{s+1-\delta}.

Note that the implicit constants in the above estimates do not depend on
\varepsilon
.

Now similar to the proof of Lemma
B.12
, since on the set
{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa
with
\kappa\geq 2\varepsilon
, both
\mathcal{G}_{7}(x)
and
\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)
are no longer singular, by Lebesgue differentiation theorem we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{7}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{7}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}=0.

Then together with (
B.87
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\lvert{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{7}(y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{G}_{6}(\cdot)},{\varphi(\cdot)}\big\rangle}-{\langle{\mathcal{G}_{7}\mathcal{G}_{6}},{\varphi}\rangle}\big\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\leq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{7}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{7}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\bigg\lvert\int_{{\lvert x\rvert}\geq\kappa}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathcal{G}_{7}(y)-\mathcal{G}_{7}(x))\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,dy\right)\mathcal{G}_{6}(x)\varphi(x)\,dx\bigg\rvert}\lesssim\kappa^{s+1-\delta}.

Since
\kappa
was arbitrary, we get (
B.86
).
∎

With the help of Lemma
B.33
and
B.34
, we have the following theorem.

Theorem B.35
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}]=\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]

in the distributional sense, where
\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
means the usual multiplication between
\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]
and
\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
.

Proof.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, note that

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\langle{(\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\big\langle{{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}](y)},{\phi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)}\rangle}_{y}\mathcal{F}[g_{6}](x)},{\varphi(x)}\big\rangle}_{x}

\displaystyle={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\varphi}\rangle},

where for the first equality since
g_{7},g_{6}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have used
[
Vla02
, Page 96]
together with Lemma
B.28
and Lemma
B.29
, for the second equality since
\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
as in
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
(
\theta_{M}
is defined in (
B.31
)) and
\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
(\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon})\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
holds as in
[
Vla02
, Page 79]
, for the third equality again since
\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\divideontimes\phi_{\varepsilon}\in\theta_{M}
,
[
Vla02
, Page 84]
is used, and for the last equality Lemma
B.34
together with Lemma
B.33
is used.
∎

Then it is worth mentioning the following corollary.

Corollary B.36
.

We have
(\mathcal{F}\circ\mathcal{F})[g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}]=\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]\right]
.

Proof.

Since
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by Theorem
B.35
we have

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}\divideontimes g_{6}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\cdot\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle}={\langle{\mathcal{F}[g_{7}]\mathcal{F}[g_{6}]},{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\rangle},

which proves the conclusion.
∎
